    temp.printAll()     // 100.0°C = 212.0°F = 373.15K | State: ♨️ Steam
    
    temp.celsius = -10.0
    temp.printAll()     // -10.0°C | State: ❄️ Ice
}

Real-World Android Example:

// Android ViewModel jaisi class — LiveData pattern simulate

class CartViewModel {

private val _items = mutableListOf<CartItem>()

data class CartItem(

val productId: Int,

val name: String,

val price: Double,

var quantity: Int = 1

)

// Read-only access to items

val items: List<CartItem>

get() = _items.toList()

// Computed properties

val itemCount: Int

get() = _items.sumOf { it.quantity }

val subtotal: Double

get() = _items.sumOf { it.price * it.quantity }

val tax: Double

get() = subtotal * 0.18 // 18% GST

val total: Double

get() = subtotal + tax

val isEmpty: Boolean

get() = _items.isEmpty()

// Methods

fun addItem(item: CartItem) {

val existing = _items.find { it.productId == item.productId }

if (existing != null) {

existing.quantity++

println("${item.name} quantity increased to ${existing.quantity}")

} else {

_items.add(item)

println("${item.name} added to cart")

}

}

fun removeItem(productId: Int) {

val removed = _items.removeAll { it.productId == productId }

if (removed) println("Item removed from cart")

}

fun printSummary() {

println("=== 🛒 Cart Summary ===")

_items.forEach { println(" ${it.name} x${it.quantity} = ₹${it.price * it.quantity}") }

println("Subtotal: ₹$subtotal | GST: ₹$tax | Total: ₹$total")

}

}

fun main() {

val cart = CartViewModel()

cart.addItem(CartViewModel.CartItem(1, "Phone", 15000.0))

cart.addItem(CartViewModel.CartItem(2, "Case", 500.0))

cart.addItem(CartViewModel.CartItem(1, "Phone", 15000.0)) // Quantity increases

cart.printSummary()

}

Real-Life Analogy

🚗 Properties = Car ke Specs (color, speed, fuel), Methods = Car ke Actions (start, brake, honk)

Custom getter = Odometer reading calculate karna har baar fresh (real-time)

Custom setter = Speed limit check karna before setting speed

Common Mistakes

Kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: Backing field use karna bhool gaye
class Wrong {
    var name: String = ""
        set(value) {

name = value // ❌ INFINITE RECURSION! setter calls setter again

}

}

// ✅ CORRECT: field keyword

class Correct {

var name: String = ""

set(value) {

field = value // ✅ field = backing field reference

}

}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: val property ko set karna

class Immutable {

val id: Int = 1

}

// val obj = Immutable()

// obj.id = 2 // ERROR! Val cannot be reassigned

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: Heavy computation in getter (called repeatedly)

class Bad {

val result: List<Int>

get() = (1..1000000).toList() // ❌ Million items created EVERY time!

}

// ✅ CORRECT: Lazy initialization

class Good {

val result: List<Int> by lazy { (1..1000000).toList() } // Only once!

}

Best Practices

val prefer karo jab property change nahi hogi

Computed properties use karo related values ke liye instead of methods with no params

Custom setters mein validation karo — fail fast

Heavy computations ke liye lazy use karo

Private properties underscore se start karo — _items, _userId

4🔒 VISIBILITY MODIFIERS

What is it?

Visibility Modifiers control karte hain ki class, property, ya method kahan se accessible hai.

Like a building ke different rooms:

🏠 public = Main door — sab andar aa sakte hain

🏠 private = Master bedroom — sirf owner andar aa sakta hai

🏠 protected = Family room — family (subclasses) andar aa sakti hai

🏠 internal = Office — same company ke log aa sakte hain

Why do we use it?

Information Hiding: Jo zarurat nahi, woh expose mat karo. Isse:

Internal implementation change ho sakti hai bina breaking changes ke

Accidental misuse prevent hoti hai

Security improve hoti hai

Syntax in Kotlin

ModifierWhere visible
public (default)Everywhere
privateSame class only
protectedSame class + subclasses
internalSame module
Kotlin
class AccessDemo {

public val publicProp = "Anyone can see" // Default

private val privateProp = "Only I can see"

protected val protectedProp = "Me + my children"

internal val internalProp = "Same module only"

public fun publicMethod() = println(publicProp)

private fun privateMethod() = println(privateProp)

protected fun protectedMethod() = println(protectedProp)

internal fun internalMethod() = println(internalProp)

}

Examples

Basic Example:

Kotlin
class Person(

val name: String, // public — bahar se access kar sakte hain

private val aadhaar: String, // private — sirf class ke andar

protected val age: Int // protected — subclasses mein bhi

) {

private var loginAttempts = 0

fun greet() {

println("Hello, I'm $name")

// Can access private inside class

println("Aadhaar (private): $aadhaar")

}

private fun logActivity() {

loginAttempts++

println("Login attempt #$loginAttempts")

}

fun login(pin: String): Boolean {

logActivity() // private method — callable inside

return pin == "1234"

}

}

class Employee(name: String, aadhaar: String, age: Int) : Person(name, aadhaar, age) {

fun showAge() {

println("Age: $age") // ✅ protected — accessible in subclass

// println(aadhaar) // ❌ private — NOT accessible

}

}

fun main() {

val p = Person("Rahul", "1234-5678-9012", 25)

println(p.name) // ✅ public

// println(p.aadhaar) // ❌ private — ERROR

// println(p.age) // ❌ protected — accessible only in subclass

p.greet()

p.login("1234")

}

Real-World Example:

Kotlin
class AuthService {
    private val validApiKeys = setOf("key_abc123", "key_xyz789")
    private var requestCount = 0
    
    internal fun validateKey(apiKey: String): Boolean {
        requestCount++
        return apiKey in validApiKeys
    }
    
    private fun logRequest(endpoint: String) {
        println("[LOG] Request #$requestCount to $endpoint")
    }
    
    fun handleRequest(apiKey: String, endpoint: String): String {
        logRequest(endpoint)  // private — internal use only
        
        return if (validateKey(apiKey)) {  // internal method
            "✅ Request authorized for $endpoint"
        } else {
            "❌ Unauthorized access attempt!"
        }
    }
}

fun main() {
    val auth = AuthService()
    println(auth.handleRequest("key_abc123", "/api/users"))   // ✅
    println(auth.handleRequest("invalid_key", "/api/data"))   // ❌
}

Common Mistakes

Kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: Sab kuch public rakhna
class Mistake {

var internalState = "should be private" // Leakage!

var helperCount = 0 // Implementation detail!

}

// ✅ CORRECT

class Correct {

private var internalState = "protected"

private var helperCount = 0

fun doWork() {

internalState = "working"

helperCount++

}

}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Protected ko public samajhna

open class Parent {

protected val secret = "only family"

}

fun main() {

val p = Parent()

// println(p.secret) // ❌ ERROR — protected not accessible from outside

}

Interview Questions

Basic:

Q: Kotlin ka default visibility modifier kya hai?
A: public — agar koi modifier nahi likha toh automatically public.

Advanced:

Q: internal modifier kab use karte hain?
A: Jab ek module (library/SDK) ke andar sab jagah accessible chahiye, lekin library use karne wale users ko expose nahi karna. Android library development mein common hai.

5🔐 ENCAPSULATION

What is it?

Encapsulation = Data + Methods ko ek class mein bundle karna + Data ko private rakhna + Controlled access dena via methods.

Capsule = Dawaai (medicine)

Tum tablet ke andar ki chemistry nahi dekhte, bas tablet lete ho.

Waise hi Encapsulation mein — internal data chupaao, clean interface do.

Why do we use it?

Data Protection — Koi bhi raw data directly change na kar sake

Validation — Data set karne se pehle check kar sako

Flexibility — Internal implementation change karo bina outside code todhe

Abstraction support — Complexity chupaao

How it Works Internally

Without Encapsulation:

account.balance = -50000 // Direct access — disaster!

With Encapsulation:

account.deposit(-50000) // Method validates: "Amount must be positive!"

Examples

Basic Encapsulation:

Kotlin
class BankAccount(
    val accountNumber: String,
    private var _balance: Double = 0.0,
    private var _pin: String
) {
    // Read-only property
    val balance: Double
        get() = _balance
    
    // Controlled deposit
    fun deposit(amount: Double): Boolean {
        if (amount <= 0) {
            println("❌ Invalid deposit amount!")
            return false
        }
        _balance += amount
        println("✅ Deposited ₹$amount | Balance: ₹$_balance")
        return true
    }
    
    // Controlled withdrawal with PIN
    fun withdraw(amount: Double, pin: String): Boolean {
        if (!verifyPin(pin)) {
            println("❌ Wrong PIN!")
            return false
        }
        if (amount > _balance) {
            println("❌ Insufficient balance!")
            return false
        }
        if (amount <= 0) {
            println("❌ Invalid amount!")
            return false
        }
        _balance -= amount
        println("✅ Withdrew ₹$amount | Remaining: ₹$_balance")
        return true
    }
    
    // PIN change with old PIN verification
    fun changePin(oldPin: String, newPin: String): Boolean {
        if (!verifyPin(oldPin)) {
            println("❌ Wrong old PIN!")
            return false
        }
        if (newPin.length != 4 || !newPin.all { it.isDigit() }) {
            println("❌ PIN must be 4 digits!")
            return false
        }
        _pin = newPin
        println("✅ PIN changed successfully!")
        return true
    }
    
    private fun verifyPin(pin: String) = pin == _pin
    
    override fun toString() = "Account[$accountNumber] Balance: ₹$_balance"
}

fun main() {
    val acc = BankAccount("SBI001", 10000.0, "1234")
    
    acc.deposit(5000.0)           // ✅ Deposited ₹5000
    acc.deposit(-100.0)           // ❌ Invalid
    acc.withdraw(3000.0, "1234")  // ✅ Withdrew
    acc.withdraw(20000.0, "1234") // ❌ Insufficient
    acc.withdraw(1000.0, "9999")  // ❌ Wrong PIN

// acc._balance = 1000000.0 // ❌ Not possible! Encapsulated!

println(acc.balance) // ✅ Read-only access

acc.changePin("1234", "5678")

acc.withdraw(500.0, "5678") // ✅ New PIN works

}

Real-World Example — User Profile:

Kotlin
class UserProfile(email: String, username: String) {
    
    private var _email: String = ""
    private var _username: String = ""
    private var _followersCount: Int = 0
    private val _posts = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    // Validated email property
    var email: String
        get() = _email
        set(value) {
            require(value.contains("@") && value.contains(".")) { "Invalid email!" }
            _email = value.lowercase()
        }
    
    // Validated username
    var username: String
        get() = _username
        set(value) {
            require(value.length in 3..20) { "Username must be 3-20 chars!" }
            require(value.all { it.isLetterOrDigit() || it == '_' }) { "Invalid chars!" }
            _username = value.lowercase()
        }
    
    // Read-only computed
    val followersCount: Int get() = _followersCount
    val postCount: Int get() = _posts.size
    val posts: List<String> get() = _posts.toList()
    
    init {
        this.email = email         // Triggers validation!
        this.username = username   // Triggers validation!
    }
    
    fun addPost(content: String) {
        require(content.isNotBlank()) { "Post cannot be empty!" }
        require(content.length <= 2200) { "Post too long!" }
        _posts.add(content)
        println("✅ Post added! Total: $postCount posts")
    }
    
    fun gainFollower() {
        _followersCount++
        // Can't be manipulated from outside!
    }
    
    fun printProfile() {
        println("""
            👤 @$_username | 📧 $_email
            Posts: $postCount | Followers: $followersCount
        """.trimIndent())
    }
}

fun main() {
    val user = UserProfile("Rahul@Gmail.Com", "Rahul_Dev")
    user.addPost("My first Kotlin post! 🎉")
    user.gainFollower()
    user.printProfile()

// user._followersCount = 1000000 // ❌ Can't cheat!

// user._posts.clear() // ❌ Can't access!

}

Real-Life Analogy

🏧 ATM Machine = Perfect Encapsulation Example

Internal mechanism (cash counting, network calls) = Private

PIN validation = Private method

Cash dispenser slot = Public interface

Tum ATM ke andar haath nahi daal sakte — sirf slot se kaam karo!

Interview Questions

Tricky:

Q: Kotlin mein data class ke properties automatically public rehti hain — kya yeh encapsulation violate karta hai?
A: Depends on use case. Data classes mostly DTOs (Data Transfer Objects) ke liye hote hain jahan data expose karna intended hai. For sensitive classes, normal class with private properties + getters prefer karo.

6👨‍👧 INHERITANCE

What is it?

Inheritance ek mechanism hai jisme ek class (child/subclass) doosri class (parent/superclass) ki properties aur methods inherit (lena) karti hai.

Animal (Parent)

├── Dog (Child) — inherits: name, eat(), sleep()
├── Cat (Child) — inherits: name, eat(), sleep()
└── Bird (Child) — inherits: name, eat(), sleep()

Sab animals apne extra features add karte hain (bark, meow, fly)

Why do we use it?

Problem: Without inheritance, har class mein same code likhna padega:

// Without inheritance — code duplication nightmare!

class Dog { var name = ""; fun eat() = println("Eating"); fun bark() = println("Woof") }

class Cat { var name = ""; fun eat() = println("Eating"); fun meow() = println("Meow") }

class Bird { var name = ""; fun eat() = println("Eating"); fun fly() = println("Flying") }

// eat() ek baar fix karo, sab jagah fix ho — not possible!

Solution: Inheritance with open keyword!

How it Works Internally

┌─────────────────────┐
│   Animal (Parent)   │
│   name: String      │
│   eat()             │
│   sleep()           │
└──────────┬──────────┘
           │ extends
    ┌──────┴──────┐
    │             │
┌───▼───┐    ┌───▼───┐
│  Dog  │    │  Cat  │
│ bark()│    │meow() │
│ fetch()    │purr() │
└───────┘    └───────┘

Dog object mein BOTH Dog + Animal members hain

Syntax in Kotlin

Kotlin
// Kotlin mein classes by default FINAL hoti hain (not inheritable)
// open keyword se inheritable banate hain

open class Animal(val name: String) {
    open fun sound() { println("$name makes a sound") }  // open = overridable
    fun eat() { println("$name is eating") }              // not open = NOT overridable
}

class Dog(name: String) : Animal(name) {
    override fun sound() { println("$name says: Woof!") }  // override
    fun fetch() { println("$name fetches the ball!") }
}

Examples

Basic Example:

Kotlin
open class Vehicle(
    val brand: String,
    val model: String,
    protected var speed: Int = 0
) {
    open val fuelType: String = "Petrol"
    
    open fun accelerate(by: Int) {
        speed += by
        println("$brand $model accelerating to $speed km/h")
    }
    
    open fun brake() {
        speed = maxOf(0, speed - 20)
        println("$brand $model braking! Speed: $speed km/h")
    }
    
    fun describe() = "$brand $model ($fuelType) | Speed: $speed"
}

class ElectricCar(brand: String, model: String) : Vehicle(brand, model) {
    override val fuelType: String = "Electric"
    private var batteryLevel: Int = 100
    
    override fun accelerate(by: Int) {
        batteryLevel -= by / 10  // Battery drains
        super.accelerate(by)     // Call parent method!
        println("Battery: $batteryLevel%")
    }
    
    fun charge() {
        batteryLevel = 100
        println("$brand $model fully charged! 🔋")
    }
}

class Truck(brand: String, model: String) : Vehicle(brand, model) {
    var cargoWeight: Int = 0
    
    override fun accelerate(by: Int) {
        val actual = if (cargoWeight > 5000) by / 2 else by  // Heavy cargo = slow
        super.accelerate(actual)
        if (cargoWeight > 5000) println("⚠️ Heavy cargo — reduced acceleration!")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val tesla = ElectricCar("Tesla", "Model 3")
    tesla.accelerate(50)
    tesla.accelerate(30)
    tesla.brake()
    println(tesla.describe())
    
    val truck = Truck("Tata", "Prima")
    truck.cargoWeight = 8000
    truck.accelerate(40)
}
Multi-Level Inheritance:
Kotlin
open class Shape {
    open fun area(): Double = 0.0
    open fun perimeter(): Double = 0.0
    fun describe() = "${this::class.simpleName}: Area=${area()}, Perimeter=${perimeter()}"
}

open class Polygon(val sides: Int) : Shape() {
    open val sideLength: Double = 0.0
}

class Rectangle(val width: Double, val height: Double) : Shape() {
    override fun area() = width * height
    override fun perimeter() = 2 * (width + height)
}

class Square(val side: Double) : Rectangle(side, side) {  // Square is Rectangle!
    override fun area() = side * side
    override fun perimeter() = 4 * side
}

class Circle(val radius: Double) : Shape() {
    override fun area() = Math.PI * radius * radius
    override fun perimeter() = 2 * Math.PI * radius  // circumference
}

fun main() {
    val shapes: List<Shape> = listOf(
        Rectangle(4.0, 6.0),
        Square(5.0),
        Circle(3.0)
    )
    shapes.forEach { println(it.describe()) }
}

Real-World Android Example:

Kotlin
// Android Fragment hierarchy simulate karna
open class BaseFragment(val fragmentName: String) {
    protected var isVisible: Boolean = false
    protected val tag: String = fragmentName.uppercase()
    
    open fun onCreate() {
        println("[$tag] onCreate() called")
    }
    
    open fun onResume() {
        isVisible = true
        println("[$tag] onResume() — Fragment visible")
    }
    
    open fun onPause() {
        isVisible = false
        println("[$tag] onPause() — Fragment hidden")
    }
    
    fun onDestroy() {
        println("[$tag] onDestroy() — Fragment destroyed")
    }
}

class HomeFragment : BaseFragment("HomeFragment") {
    private val posts = mutableListOf<String>()
    
    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()  // ← Always call super!
        println("[$tag] Loading home feed...")
        posts.addAll(listOf("Post 1", "Post 2", "Post 3"))
    }
    
    override fun onResume() {
        super.onResume()
        println("[$tag] Refreshing ${posts.size} posts")
    }
    
    fun loadMorePosts() = println("[$tag] Loading more posts...")
}

class ProfileFragment(val userId: Int) : BaseFragment("ProfileFragment") {
    
    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()
        println("[$tag] Loading profile for user $userId...")
    }
    
    fun editProfile() = println("[$tag] Opening edit profile...")
}

fun main() {
    val home = HomeFragment()
    home.onCreate()
    home.onResume()
    home.onPause()
    
    val profile = ProfileFragment(42)
    profile.onCreate()
    profile.editProfile()
}

Real-Life Analogy

👨‍👩‍👧 Inheritance = Biological Inheritance

Tum apne parents se kuch cheezein inherit karte ho — aankhon ka color, height, nose shape (properties). Lekin tum apne khud ke skills bhi add karte ho — coding, cooking (methods). Yahi hai OOP inheritance! super.onCreate() = "Pehle papa wala kaam karo, phir apna karo"

Common Mistakes

Kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: open keyword bhool gaya
class Animal { fun eat() = println("Eating") }
// class Dog : Animal() { }  // ERROR! Animal is final by default

// ✅
open class Animal { open fun eat() = println("Eating") }

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Constructor chaining bhool gaya
open class Person(val name: String)
// class Student : Person { }  // ERROR! Must call Person constructor

// ✅
class Student(name: String, val grade: Int) : Person(name)

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: Super call bhool gaya (when needed)
open class Base {
    open fun doWork() {
        println("Base doing important initialization work")
    }
}
class Derived : Base() {
    override fun doWork() {
        // super.doWork()  // ❌ Forgot super — parent work not done!
        println("Derived work only")
    }
}

// ✅ Always consider if super should be called

Best Practices

Composition over Inheritance — Jab possible ho, interface ya composition prefer karo

super.method() call karo jab parent ka kaam important ho

Inheritance ki depth 2-3 levels tak rakho — zyada deep = confusing

Liskov Substitution Principle — Child class parent ki jagah use ho sake

override keyword clearly likho — compiler enforce karta hai

Interview Questions

Basic:

Q: Kotlin mein multiple inheritance kyu nahi hai?
A: Diamond problem ki wajah se — agar A aur B dono ka fun() ho, aur C dono se inherit kare, toh C.fun() kaun sa use kare? Solution: Interfaces use karo (multiple interfaces allowed, single class inheritance).

Advanced:

Q: Kotlin classes default final kyun hain?
A: Effective Java principle — "Design for inheritance or prohibit it." Unintended inheritance se bugs aate hain. open explicitly likhne se conscious decision hoti hai.

7🎭 POLYMORPHISM

What is it?

Polymorphism = "Poly" (many) + "Morph" (forms) = Ek cheez ke anek roop

Ek method ka naam same, behavior alag alag — caller ko tension nahi ki konsi exact implementation hai.

sound() method:

- Dog.sound() → "Woof!"

- Cat.sound() → "Meow!"

- Bird.sound() → "Tweet!"

Caller: animal.sound() — bina jaane ki woh Dog/Cat/Bird hai

Two Types:

Compile-Time Polymorphism (Static Dispatch) = Method Overloading — same naam, alag parameters

Runtime Polymorphism (Dynamic Dispatch) = Method Overriding — parent reference, child implementation

How it Works Internally

Runtime Polymorphism mein JVM vtable (virtual method table) use karta hai:

Har object ke saath ek hidden pointer hota hai vtable ki taraf

Vtable mein overridden methods ki actual implementation pointers hote hain

animal.sound() call hota hai → vtable se actual Dog.sound() pointer milta hai → Dog.sound() execute

Animal vtable: Dog vtable:

┌──────────┐          ┌──────────┐
│ sound()  │──────▶   │ sound()  │──▶ Dog's sound implementation
│ eat()    │          │ eat()    │──▶ Animal's eat (not overridden)
└──────────┘          └──────────┘

Examples

Compile-Time Polymorphism (Overloading):

Kotlin
class Calculator {
    // Same name, different parameters
    fun add(a: Int, b: Int): Int = a + b
    fun add(a: Double, b: Double): Double = a + b
    fun add(a: Int, b: Int, c: Int): Int = a + b + c
    fun add(a: String, b: String): String = a + b  // String concat
    
    fun format(value: Int) = "Integer: $value"
    fun format(value: Double) = "Double: ${"%.2f".format(value)}"
    fun format(value: String) = "String: $value"
}

fun main() {
    val calc = Calculator()
    println(calc.add(5, 3))           // 8 (Int version)
    println(calc.add(5.5, 3.2))       // 8.7 (Double version)
    println(calc.add(1, 2, 3))        // 6 (3-param version)
    println(calc.add("Hello", " World")) // Hello World (String)
    println(calc.format(42))          // Integer: 42
    println(calc.format(3.14))        // Double: 3.14
}

Runtime Polymorphism (Overriding):

Kotlin
open class Notification(val title: String, val message: String) {
    open fun send() {
        println("📩 Sending notification: $title — $message")
    }
    
    open fun getIcon(): String = "🔔"
    
    fun display() {
        println("${getIcon()} $title: $message")
    }
}

class EmailNotification(
    title: String,
    message: String,
    private val recipient: String
) : Notification(title, message) {
    override fun send() {
        println("📧 Email to $recipient: [$title] $message")
    }
    override fun getIcon() = "📧"
}

class SMSNotification(
    title: String,
    message: String,
    private val phoneNumber: String
) : Notification(title, message) {
    override fun send() {
        println("📱 SMS to $phoneNumber: $message")
    }
    override fun getIcon() = "📱"
}

class PushNotification(
    title: String,
    message: String,
    private val deviceToken: String
) : Notification(title, message) {
    override fun send() {
        println("🔔 Push to device[$deviceToken]: $title — $message")
    }
    override fun getIcon() = "🔔"
}

// Polymorphic function — doesn't care which type!
fun sendAll(notifications: List<Notification>) {
    println("=== Sending ${notifications.size} notifications ===")
    notifications.forEach { notification ->
        notification.send()  // Runtime decides which send() to call!
        notification.display()
    }
}

fun main() {
    val notifications: List<Notification> = listOf(  // Parent type reference!
        EmailNotification("Welcome!", "Thank you for joining", "user@email.com"),
        SMSNotification("OTP", "Your OTP is 123456", "+91-9876543210"),
        PushNotification("Sale Alert", "50% off today!", "device_token_xyz"),
        EmailNotification("Invoice", "Your invoice is ready", "user@email.com")
    )
    
    sendAll(notifications)  // Polymorphism magic!
}

Real-World Android — Payment Polymorphism:

Kotlin
abstract class PaymentMethod(
    val name: String,
    protected var amount: Double
) {
    abstract fun processPayment(): Boolean
    abstract fun getReceiptDetails(): String
    
    open fun validateAmount(): Boolean {
        return amount > 0
    }
    
    fun pay(): Boolean {
        println("💳 Processing ₹$amount via $name...")
        if (!validateAmount()) {
            println("❌ Invalid amount!")
            return false
        }
        val success = processPayment()
        if (success) {
            println("✅ Payment successful!")
            println(getReceiptDetails())
        }
        return success
    }
}

class UPIPayment(amount: Double, private val upiId: String) : PaymentMethod("UPI", amount) {
    override fun processPayment(): Boolean {
        println("  Sending to UPI ID: $upiId")
        return upiId.contains("@")  // Basic validation
    }
    override fun getReceiptDetails() = "UPI Ref: UPI${System.currentTimeMillis()}"
}

class CreditCardPayment(
    amount: Double,
    private val cardNumber: String,
    private val cvv: String
) : PaymentMethod("Credit Card", amount) {
    override fun processPayment(): Boolean {
        println("  Processing card: xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-${cardNumber.takeLast(4)}")
        return cardNumber.length == 16 && cvv.length == 3
    }
    override fun validateAmount() = amount > 0 && amount <= 100000.0  // Card limit
    override fun getReceiptDetails() = "Card Auth: AUTH${(1000..9999).random()}"
}

class WalletPayment(amount: Double, private var walletBalance: Double) : PaymentMethod("Wallet", amount) {
    override fun processPayment(): Boolean {
        if (amount > walletBalance) {
            println("  ❌ Insufficient wallet balance! Balance: ₹$walletBalance")
            return false
        }
        walletBalance -= amount
        println("  Wallet balance remaining: ₹$walletBalance")
        return true
    }
    override fun getReceiptDetails() = "Wallet TXN: WLT${System.currentTimeMillis()}"
}

fun checkout(payment: PaymentMethod) {  // Polymorphic — takes any payment!
    payment.pay()
}

fun main() {
    val payments: List<PaymentMethod> = listOf(
        UPIPayment(500.0, "rahul@okaxis"),
        CreditCardPayment(15000.0, "1234567890123456", "123"),
        WalletPayment(200.0, 150.0),  // Insufficient balance case
        WalletPayment(100.0, 500.0)
    )
    
    payments.forEach { payment ->
        println("\n--- ${payment.name} Payment ---")
        checkout(payment)  // Same function, different behavior!
    }
}

Real-Life Analogy

🎮 Remote Control = Polymorphism

Teri TV remote ka "Volume Up" button:

Sony TV mein → Sony ka volume system chalata hai

Samsung mein → Samsung ka volume system

LG mein → LG ka volume system

Button same hai, behavior alag! Yahi hai runtime polymorphism.

Interview Questions

Tricky:

Q: Method overloading compile-time kyun hai?
A: Because at compile time, compiler arguments ke types se decide kar leta hai konsa method call hoga. No runtime decision needed.
Q: Kotlin mein Any.equals() ka example do polymorphism ka?
A: equals() har class mein override hota hai differently — String.equals() characters compare karta hai, Int.equals() values compare karta hai, custom classes apna equals() define karte hain.

8🎭 ABSTRACTION

What is it?

Abstraction = "Kya karta hai" dikhao, "Kaise karta hai" chupaao.

Car chalate waqt:

✅Tum jaante ho: Steering ghuma → car mudti hai
❌Tum nahi jaante: Steering column, rack-pinion mechanism, hydraulic fluid

Steering wheel = Abstract Interface

Internal mechanism = Hidden Implementation

Why do we use it?

Complexity reduce karo — Users ko sirf relevant info chahiye

Maintainability — Implementation change karo bina interface todhe

Security — Dangerous internals expose mat karo

Team work — Team members parallel kaam kar sakti hain interfaces define karke

Abstraction Achieve Karna:

Abstract Classes — Partial abstraction

Interfaces — Full abstraction

9🏛️ ABSTRACT CLASSES

What is it?

Abstract class ek incomplete class hai jise directly instantiate nahi kar sakte. Isko inherit karna padta hai aur abstract methods implement karne padte hain.

Abstract class = Incomplete blueprint

Concrete class = Complete implementation

Syntax in Kotlin

Kotlin
abstract class Shape {
    // Abstract property — must be overridden
    abstract val color: String
    
    // Abstract method — no body, must be overridden
    abstract fun area(): Double
    abstract fun perimeter(): Double
    
    // Concrete method — has body, inherited as-is
    fun describe() {
        println("${this::class.simpleName}: color=$color, area=${area()}, perimeter=${perimeter()}")
    }

// Concrete method that calls abstract — Template Method Pattern!

fun draw() {

println("Drawing $color shape")

setupCanvas()

render()

applyColor()

}

private fun setupCanvas() = println("Canvas setup done")

protected abstract fun render()

private fun applyColor() = println("Applying color: $color")

}

class Circle(override val color: String, val radius: Double) : Shape() {

override fun area() = Math.PI radius radius

override fun perimeter() = 2 Math.PI radius

override fun render() = println("Rendering circle with radius $radius")

}

class Rectangle(override val color: String, val w: Double, val h: Double) : Shape() {

override fun area() = w * h

override fun perimeter() = 2 * (w + h)

override fun render() = println("Rendering rectangle ${w}x${h}")

}

Examples

Real-World Example — Repository Pattern:

Kotlin
// Abstract Repository — Database ka abstraction
abstract class BaseRepository<T, ID> {
    protected val cache = mutableMapOf<ID, T>()

// Abstract — subclass decide karega kaise implement karna

abstract fun findById(id: ID): T?

abstract fun save(entity: T): Boolean

abstract fun delete(id: ID): Boolean

abstract fun findAll(): List<T>

// Concrete — same for all

fun existsById(id: ID): Boolean {

return findById(id) != null

}

fun count(): Int = findAll().size

fun saveAll(entities: List<T>): Int {

return entities.count { save(it) }

}

}

data class User(val id: Int, val name: String, val email: String)

class UserRepository : BaseRepository<User, Int>() {

override fun findById(id: Int): User? {

// Real app mein: database query

return cache[id]

}

override fun save(entity: User): Boolean {

return try {

// Validation

require(entity.name.isNotBlank()) { "Name required" }

require(entity.email.contains("@")) { "Valid email required" }

cache[entity.id] = entity

println("✅ User saved: ${entity.name}")

true

} catch (e: IllegalArgumentException) {

println("❌ Save failed: ${e.message}")

false

}

}

override fun delete(id: Int): Boolean {

return if (cache.containsKey(id)) {

cache.remove(id)

println("✅ User $id deleted")

true

} else {

println("❌ User $id not found")

false

}

}

override fun findAll(): List<User> = cache.values.toList()

// Extra user-specific method

fun findByEmail(email: String): User? {

return cache.values.find { it.email == email }

}

}

fun main() {

val repo = UserRepository()

repo.save(User(1, "Rahul", "rahul@test.com"))

repo.save(User(2, "Priya", "priya@test.com"))

repo.save(User(3, "", "invalid")) // Will fail validation

println("Total users: ${repo.count()}")

println("User 1 exists: ${repo.existsById(1)}")

println("Find by email: ${repo.findByEmail("priya@test.com")}")

repo.delete(1)

println("All users: ${repo.findAll()}")

}

Abstract vs Concrete — When to Use Which?

Use Abstract Class when:

✅Common base implementation share karni ho
✅Template Method Pattern chahiye (skeleton define karo)
✅Constructor chahiye abstract type mein
✅State (properties) abstract type mein share karni ho

Use Concrete Class when:

✅Complete implementation hai
✅Directly instantiate karna ho

Interview Questions

Advanced:

Q: Abstract class ka constructor ho sakta hai? Agar haan toh kaisa use karta hai?
A: Haan! Abstract class ka constructor ho sakta hai — lekin directly nahi call hota. Jab subclass instantiated hoti hai, uska constructor super() through abstract class ka constructor call karta hai. Initialization kaam ke liye useful hai.

10📋 INTERFACES

What is it?

Interface ek contract hai — "Jo bhi mujhe implement karega, usse ye sab methods provide karne honge."

Interface = Job Description

Implementing Class = Employee who fulfills that job description

Interface: "Payment karna aana chahiye"

UPIPayment: "Haan! Mujhe UPI se payment karna aata hai"

CardPayment: "Haan! Mujhe Card se payment karna aata hai"

Key Differences from Abstract Class:

FeatureAbstract ClassInterface
State (properties)YesLimited (no backing field by default)
ConstructorYesNo
Multiple inheritanceNoYes (multiple interfaces)
Access modifiersAllpublic by default
Default implementationYes (from Kotlin)Yes (from Kotlin)

Syntax in Kotlin

Kotlin
interface Drawable {

val color: String // Abstract property — no backing field

fun draw() // Abstract method

fun clear() { // Default implementation (Kotlin feature!)

println("Clearing $color drawing")

}

}

interface Resizable {

fun resize(factor: Double)

fun getSize(): Pair<Double, Double>

}

// Multiple interface implementation!

class Canvas(override val color: String) : Drawable, Resizable {

private var width = 100.0

private var height = 100.0

override fun draw() = println("Drawing on $color canvas (${width}x${height})")

override fun resize(factor: Double) {

width *= factor

height *= factor

println("Resized to ${width}x${height}")

}

override fun getSize() = Pair(width, height)

}

Examples

Comprehensive Interface Example:

Kotlin
interface Authenticatable {
    fun login(username: String, password: String): Boolean
    fun logout()
    fun isLoggedIn(): Boolean
    
    // Default implementation
    fun getSessionInfo(): String = "Session active: ${isLoggedIn()}"
}

interface Auditable {
    val createdAt: Long
    val createdBy: String
    var updatedAt: Long
    
    fun getAuditLog(): String = "Created: $createdAt by $createdBy | Updated: $updatedAt"
}

interface Serializable {
    fun toJson(): String
    fun fromJson(json: String): Any
}

// Class implementing multiple interfaces
class UserService(
    override val createdBy: String
) : Authenticatable, Auditable {
    
    override val createdAt: Long = System.currentTimeMillis()
    override var updatedAt: Long = createdAt
    
    private var _isLoggedIn = false
    private var currentUser: String? = null
    private val validUsers = mapOf("rahul" to "pass123", "priya" to "pass456")
    
    override fun login(username: String, password: String): Boolean {
        return if (validUsers[username] == password) {
            _isLoggedIn = true
            currentUser = username
            updatedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
            println("✅ $username logged in successfully")
            true
        } else {
            println("❌ Invalid credentials for $username")
            false
        }
    }
    
    override fun logout() {
        println("👋 $currentUser logged out")
        _isLoggedIn = false
        currentUser = null
        updatedAt = System.currentTimeMillis()
    }
    
    override fun isLoggedIn() = _isLoggedIn
    
    fun getCurrentUser() = currentUser
}

fun main() {
    val service = UserService("System")
    
    println(service.getAuditLog())
    
    service.login("rahul", "wrongpass")  // ❌
    service.login("rahul", "pass123")    // ✅
    
    println(service.getSessionInfo())  // Default implementation
    println("Current user: ${service.getCurrentUser()}")
    
    service.logout()
    println(service.getSessionInfo())
}

Real-World Android-style Interface:

Kotlin
// Callback interfaces — Android mein bohot common
interface OnItemClickListener {
    fun onItemClick(position: Int, itemId: Long)
    fun onItemLongClick(position: Int): Boolean = false  // Default = no action
}

interface NetworkCallback {
    fun onSuccess(response: String)
    fun onError(error: String)
    fun onLoading(isLoading: Boolean) { }  // Default empty
}

interface LifecycleOwner {
    fun onStart()
    fun onStop()
    fun onDestroy()
}

// Simulating an Android Activity
class MainActivity : LifecycleOwner, OnItemClickListener {
    private val items = listOf("Product A", "Product B", "Product C")
    
    override fun onStart() {
        println("🟢 MainActivity started")
        loadData()
    }
    
    override fun onStop() = println("🟡 MainActivity stopped")
    override fun onDestroy() = println("🔴 MainActivity destroyed")
    
    override fun onItemClick(position: Int, itemId: Long) {
        println("Clicked: ${items[position]} (id: $itemId)")
        navigateToDetail(itemId)
    }
    
    override fun onItemLongClick(position: Int): Boolean {
        println("Long clicked: ${items[position]} — showing options menu")
        return true  // Event consumed
    }
    
    private fun loadData() {
        val networkCallback = object : NetworkCallback {
            override fun onSuccess(response: String) {
                println("✅ Data loaded: $response")
            }
            override fun onError(error: String) {
                println("❌ Error: $error")
            }
            override fun onLoading(isLoading: Boolean) {
                println("Loading: $isLoading")
            }
        }
        
        networkCallback.onLoading(true)
        networkCallback.onSuccess("3 products fetched")
        networkCallback.onLoading(false)
    }
    
    private fun navigateToDetail(id: Long) = println("Navigating to detail: $id")
}

fun main() {
    val activity = MainActivity()
    activity.onStart()
    activity.onItemClick(1, 102L)
    activity.onItemLongClick(0)
    activity.onStop()
    activity.onDestroy()
}

Real-Life Analogy

📄 Interface = Electrical Socket Standard

India mein 3-pin socket standard hai — ye ek interface hai.

Koi bhi company fan, AC, fridge bana sakti hai — bas unhe 3-pin plug (implement interface) lagana hai.

Socket ko nahi pata ki fan hai ya AC — woh sirf standard follow karta hai.

Common Mistakes

Kotlin
// ❌ MISTAKE 1: Interface mein state rakhna
interface Wrong {
    var count = 0  // ERROR! Interface properties can't have initializers (unless companion)
}

// ✅ CORRECT — Abstract property
interface Correct {

var count: Int // Abstract — implementing class provides state

}

// ❌ MISTAKE 2: Interface instance banana

// val x = Drawable() // ERROR!

// ✅ CORRECT: Implement karo ya anonymous object

val x = object : Drawable {

override val color = "Blue"

override fun draw() = println("Drawing")

}

// ❌ MISTAKE 3: Diamond problem ignore karna

interface A { fun hello() = println("A") }

interface B { fun hello() = println("B") }

class C : A, B {

// override fun hello() = ??? // MUST override when ambiguous!

override fun hello() {

super<A>.hello() // Specify which one!

}

}

Interview Questions

Advanced:

Q: Interface mein companion object kab use karte hain?
A: Factory methods define karne ke liye, ya interface-level constants ke liye:
Kotlin
interface Config {
    companion object {
        const val MAX_RETRY = 3
        const val TIMEOUT = 30000L
        fun create(): Config = DefaultConfig()  // Factory
    }
}
🏗️

Chapter 3: OOP Concepts Deep Dive

18 core OOP concepts — Hinglish explanations, real-world analogies, code examples

#1
CLASS & OBJECT

🧒 Simple Explanation — 10 Saal ke Bachche ko Samjhao

Socho tumhare paas ek biscuit ka mould (cutter) hai — jaise star shape wala.

Mould = Class (yeh ek design hai, ek plan hai)

Biscuit = Object (yeh real cheez hai jo mould se banti hai)

Ek mould se hazaron biscuits ban sakte hain. Waise hi ek class se hazaron objects ban sakte hain!

CLASS (Mould) OBJECTS (Biscuits)

┌──────────────┐           ┌──────┐  ┌──────┐  ┌──────┐
│  🍪 Design   │  ───────▶ │ 🍪1  │  │ 🍪2  │  │ 🍪3  │
│  Shape: Star │           │Choco │  │Vanil │  │Straw │
│  Size: 5cm   │           │5cm   │  │5cm   │  │5cm   │
└──────────────┘           └──────┘  └──────┘  └──────┘

🌍 Real-Life Example

Mobile phone ka example lo!

Samsung ne ek design banaya — Galaxy S24 ka blueprint:

Screen size: 6.2 inch

Battery: 4000mAh

Camera: 50MP

Yeh blueprint = Class

Is blueprint se lakhs phones banaye — har phone alag color ka, alag storage ka. Har ek phone = Object

📱 SmartPhone (Class) Samsung S24 objects:

─────────────────          ─────────────────────────
screenSize: Float    ───▶  phone1: color=Black, 128GB

battery: Int phone2: color=White, 256GB

camera: Int phone3: color=Blue, 512GB

makeCall()

takePicture()

🤔 Why Ye Exist Karta Hai? (Problem Solve)

Problem without class:

Maan lo tumhare paas 100 students hain.

Bina class ke tumhe likhna padega:

student1Name = "Rahul"

student1Age = 20

student1Grade = "A"

student2Name = "Priya"

student2Age = 21

student2Grade = "B"

...100 students tak... 😫 300 variables!

Class se solution:

Kotlin
class Student {
    var name = ""
    var age = 0
    var grade = ""
}

// Bas ek class, aur 100 objects!
val s1 = Student()   // Rahul
val s2 = Student()   // Priya
// ... aur 98 aur!
⚙️ Internally Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?

Jab tum likhte ho: val dog = Dog()

Computer ke andar:

┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  STACK MEMORY        HEAP MEMORY        │
│  ┌──────────┐       ┌──────────────┐   │
│  │ dog ─────┼──────▶│ Dog Object   │   │
│  │ (address)│       │ name: ""     │   │
│  └──────────┘       │ age: 0       │   │
│                     └──────────────┘   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘

dog variable = address ka number (jaise ghar ka GPS location)

Actual dog object = Heap memory mein rehta hai

Simple language mein:

dog ek remote control hai

Actual dog object ek TV hai (Heap mein)

Remote control TV ko point karta hai!

💻 Kotlin Syntax

Kotlin
// Class banane ka tarika
class ClassName {
    // Properties (data/information)
    var property1: Type = defaultValue
    
    // Methods (kaam/behavior)
    fun methodName() {
        // kya karna hai
    }
}

// Object banana
val objectName = ClassName()

// Object use karna
objectName.property1 = "value"
objectName.methodName()
🧪 Code Examples

Easy Example — Dog Class

Kotlin
// Class definition (mould/blueprint)
class Dog {
    // Properties — dog ke baare mein information
    var name: String = ""      // dog ka naam
    var breed: String = ""     // dog ki breed
    var age: Int = 0           // dog ki umar
    
    // Method — dog kya kar sakta hai
    fun bark() {
        println("$name bol raha hai: Woof! Woof! 🐕")
    }
    
    fun eat() {
        println("$name khaana kha raha hai 🍖")
    }
}

fun main() {
    // Object banana (biscuit banana mould se)
    val myDog = Dog()
    
    // Properties set karna
    myDog.name = "Bruno"
    myDog.breed = "Labrador"
    myDog.age = 3
    
    // Methods call karna
    myDog.bark()   // Bruno bol raha hai: Woof! Woof! 🐕
    myDog.eat()    // Bruno khaana kha raha hai 🍖
    
    // Doosra object!
    val friendDog = Dog()
    friendDog.name = "Max"
    friendDog.bark()   // Max bol raha hai: Woof! Woof! 🐕
}
Code line by line samjho:

class Dog { }          → Dog ka blueprint banaya

var name = "" → Ek property: naam store karne ke liye

fun bark() { } → Ek method: bhaunke ke liye

val myDog = Dog() → Ek actual dog object banaya

myDog.name = "Bruno" → Is specific dog ka naam set kiya

myDog.bark() → Is dog ko bhaunke bola

Medium Example — Student Management

Kotlin
class Student {
    var name: String = ""
    var rollNo: Int = 0
    var marks: Int = 0
    
    // Method jo marks se grade calculate kare
    fun getGrade(): String {
        return when {
            marks >= 90 -> "A+"    // 90 ya zyada = A+
            marks >= 80 -> "A"
            marks >= 70 -> "B"
            marks >= 60 -> "C"
            else -> "F"            // 60 se kam = Fail
        }
    }
    
    // Student ki info print karo
    fun printReport() {
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
        println("📋 Student Report Card")
        println("Naam: $name")
        println("Roll No: $rollNo")
        println("Marks: $marks/100")
        println("Grade: ${getGrade()}")
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val student1 = Student()
    student1.name = "Rahul Sharma"
    student1.rollNo = 101
    student1.marks = 85
    student1.printReport()
    
    val student2 = Student()
    student2.name = "Priya Singh"
    student2.rollNo = 102
    student2.marks = 92
    student2.printReport()
}

Real-World Example — Android ViewModel Style

// Android apps mein User ko aise represent karte hain

class User {

var id: Int = 0

var username: String = ""

var email: String = ""

var isLoggedIn: Boolean = false

// Login karna

fun login(inputEmail: String, inputPassword: String): Boolean {

// Real app mein yahan server check hoga

return if (inputEmail == email && inputPassword == "1234") {

isLoggedIn = true

println("✅ $username — Login successful!")

true

} else {

println("❌ Wrong credentials!")

false

}

}

fun logout() {

isLoggedIn = false

println("👋 $username — Logged out!")

}

fun getDisplayName(): String {

return if (isLoggedIn) "👤 $username" else "🚫 Not logged in"

}

}

fun main() {

val user = User()

user.id = 1

user.username = "rahul_dev"

user.email = "rahul@gmail.com"

println(user.getDisplayName()) // 🚫 Not logged in

user.login("rahul@gmail.com", "1234")

println(user.getDisplayName()) // 👤 rahul_dev

user.logout()

}

⚠️️ Common Mistakes
Kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: Object banaye bina use karna
class Cat { var name = "Kitty" }
// Cat.name  ← ERROR! Pehle object banao!

// ✅ SAHI TARIKA
val cat = Cat()
println(cat.name)  // ✅

// ❌ GALTI 2: Class ka naam lowercase mein
// class myClass { }  ← Convention galat hai!

// ✅ SAHI: PascalCase (har word capital)
class MyClass { }
class BankAccount { }
class UserProfile { }

// ❌ GALTI 3: Ek class mein bahut kuch thoodna
class EverythingClass {
    fun loginUser() {}
    fun sendEmail() {}
    fun processPayment() {}
    fun generateReport() {}
    // ← Yeh ek class ka kaam nahi hai!
}

// ✅ SAHI: Ek class = Ek kaam
class LoginService { fun loginUser() {} }
class EmailService { fun sendEmail() {} }
💡 Pro Tips
💡 TIP 1: Class ka naam noun (naam) hona chahiye
   ✅ Dog, Student, BankAccount, UserProfile
   ❌ running, doWork, processIt

💡 TIP 2: Method ka naam verb (kaam) hona chahiye
   ✅ run(), calculateGrade(), sendEmail()
   ❌ running(), grade(), email()

💡 TIP 3: Ek class 200 lines se chhoti rakho
   Zyada badi class = Zyada confusion = Bug magnet!

💡 TIP 4: Properties ko private rakho (agle concept mein seekhoge)
   Seedha access mat do — methods se karo
🎯 When NOT to Use Class
❌ Jab sirf ek simple calculation karni ho:
   fun addNumbers(a: Int, b: Int) = a + b
   (Class ki zarurat nahi!)

❌ Jab sirf ek baar use hoga koi function:

Class banane se code unnecessarily complex hoga

✅Class tab banao jab:

- Related data + behavior ek saath ho

- Multiple instances chahiye hon

- Real-world entity ko represent karna ho

🔥 Interview Questions

Q1: Class aur Object mein kya fark hai?
A: Class ek blueprint/template hai (jaise ghar ka naksha).

Object us blueprint ka ek real instance hai (jaise actual ghar).

Class = Recipe

Object = Actual Dish

Ek class se multiple objects ban sakte hain.

Class memory nahi leti — object memory leta hai.

Q2: Kotlin mein new keyword kyun nahi hai object banane ke liye?
A: Kotlin ne new keyword hataaya — cleaner syntax ke liye.

Java mein: Dog dog = new Dog();

Kotlin mein: val dog = Dog()

Internally compiler new call karta hai JVM ke liye,

hum developers ko likhna nahi padta.

🧩 Practice Question

Sawaal: Ek Car class banao jisme yeh ho:

Properties: brand, color, speed

Method accelerate() jo speed 10 se badhaye

Method describe() jo car ki info print kare

Solution:

Kotlin
class Car {
    var brand: String = ""
    var color: String = ""
    var speed: Int = 0
    
    fun accelerate() {
        speed += 10
        println("$brand ki speed ab hai: $speed km/h 🚗")
    }
    
    fun describe() {
        println("Brand: $brand | Color: $color | Speed: $speed km/h")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val myCar = Car()
    myCar.brand = "Toyota"
    myCar.color = "Red"
    
    myCar.describe()      // Brand: Toyota | Color: Red | Speed: 0 km/h
    myCar.accelerate()    // Toyota ki speed ab hai: 10 km/h
    myCar.accelerate()    // Toyota ki speed ab hai: 20 km/h
    myCar.describe()      // Brand: Toyota | Color: Red | Speed: 20 km/h
}
🧠 Quick Summary + Memory Trick
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  QUICK SUMMARY                                  │
│  • Class = Blueprint/Design/Mould               │
│  • Object = Real thing made from blueprint      │
│  • Properties = Data/Information in class       │
│  • Methods = Actions/Behavior in class          │
│  • Ek class se hazaron objects ban sakte hain   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

🧠 MEMORY TRICK:

"Class = COOKIE CUTTER, Object = COOKIE"

Ek cookie cutter se hazaron cookies!

#2
CONSTRUCTORS

🧒 Simple Explanation

Socho tumne naya phone order kiya Amazon se.

Phone delivery hone ke time already setup hota hai:

IMEI number already set hai

Model name already set hai

Color already set hai

Tum baad mein manually set nahi karte — delivery ke time hi sab set ho jaata hai.

Constructor = Delivery ke time automatic setup!

BINA CONSTRUCTOR: CONSTRUCTOR KE SAATH:

val phone = Phone() val phone = Phone("Samsung", "Black", 128)

phone.brand = "Samsung" ← Sab ek baar mein!

phone.color = "Black"

phone.storage = 128

// 3 extra lines... 😫

🌍 Real-Life Example

School admission form ki tarah socho!

Jab koi bachcha school mein admit hota hai, admission form mein sab bharte hain:

Naam

Date of Birth

Parent ka naam

Address

Yeh sab ek baar fill hota hai admission ke time — yahi hai Constructor!

Aur do tarah ke forms ho sakte hain:

Primary Form — Sabse zaroori info (naam, DOB) → Primary Constructor

Secondary Form — Extra info (sports preference, etc.) → Secondary Constructor

🤔 Why Constructor Exist Karta Hai?

Problem:

Kotlin
class Student {
    var name = ""
    var age = 0
}

val s = Student()
// Agar tum name set karna bhool gaye:
// s.age = 20
// s.printInfo()  ← Name empty rahega! Bug! 🐛
Constructor se fix:
Kotlin
class Student(val name: String, val age: Int) {

// Ab object banaoge toh name aur age mandatory hain!

}

val s = Student("Rahul", 20) // Forced to provide both!

val s2 = Student() // ❌ ERROR! Compiler nahi dega

⚙️ Internally Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?

Jab tum likhte ho: val dog = Dog("Bruno", 3)

1JVM heap mein memory allocate karta hai Dog ke liye

2Constructor run hota hai:

- name = "Bruno" set hota hai

- age = 3 set hota hai

3Object ready! Reference dog variable mein aata hai

Timeline:

Memory Allocate → Constructor Run → Object Ready → Variable mein Save

💻 Kotlin Syntax

Kotlin
// ═══════════════════════════════════════
// PRIMARY CONSTRUCTOR — Class header mein
// ═══════════════════════════════════════
class Person(val name: String, val age: Int) {

// val/var likhne se automatically property ban jaati hai!

}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════

// INIT BLOCK — Constructor ke saath chalne wala code

// ═══════════════════════════════════════

class Person(val name: String, val age: Int) {

init {

println("Person banaya gaya: $name") // Jab bhi object bane, yeh chalega

require(age > 0) { "Umar positive honi chahiye!" } // Validation

}

}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════

// SECONDARY CONSTRUCTOR — Class body mein

// ═══════════════════════════════════════

class Person(val name: String, val age: Int) {

// Secondary constructor — primary ko call karna ZAROORI hai!

constructor(name: String) : this(name, 0) {

// Sirf naam diya, age default 0 hogi

}

}

// ═══════════════════════════════════════

// DEFAULT VALUES — Most clean way!

// ═══════════════════════════════════════

class Person(

val name: String,

val age: Int = 0, // Default value!

val city: String = "Unknown"

)

val p1 = Person("Rahul", 25, "Delhi")

val p2 = Person("Priya", 22) // city = "Unknown"

val p3 = Person("Arjun") // age = 0, city = "Unknown"

🧪 Code Examples

Easy Example

Kotlin
// Primary constructor with val properties
class Dog(val name: String, val breed: String, val age: Int) {
    
    // init block — object bante hi chalta hai
    init {
        println("🐕 Naya dog register hua: $name!")
    }
    
    fun bark() {
        println("$name: Woof! Woof!")
    }
    
    fun info() {
        println("Naam: $name | Breed: $breed | Umar: $age saal")
    }
}

fun main() {
    // Object banate waqt hi sab de do
    val dog1 = Dog("Bruno", "Labrador", 3)   // ← Constructor call!
    // Output: 🐕 Naya dog register hua: Bruno!
    
    dog1.bark()   // Bruno: Woof! Woof!
    dog1.info()   // Naam: Bruno | Breed: Labrador | Umar: 3 saal
    
    val dog2 = Dog("Max", "Poodle", 5)
    // Output: 🐕 Naya dog register hua: Max!
    dog2.bark()   // Max: Woof! Woof!
}

Medium Example — Default Values + Validation

Kotlin
class BankAccount(
    val accountNumber: String,
    val holderName: String,

private var balance: Double = 0.0 // Default: khaata khali se shuru

) {

init {

// Validation — account number 10 digit ka hona chahiye

require(accountNumber.length == 10) {

"Account number 10 digits ka hona chahiye!"

}

require(holderName.isNotBlank()) {

"Naam khali nahi ho sakta!"

}

println("✅ Account khula: $holderName ka (A/C: $accountNumber)")

println(" Opening balance: ₹$balance")

}

fun deposit(amount: Double) {

balance += amount

println("✅ ₹$amount jama hua | Balance: ₹$balance")

}

fun getBalance() = balance

}

fun main() {

// Normal account — balance 0 se shuru

val acc1 = BankAccount("1234567890", "Rahul Sharma")

acc1.deposit(5000.0)

// Account with initial balance

val acc2 = BankAccount("9876543210", "Priya Singh", 10000.0)

// ❌ Galat account number

// val acc3 = BankAccount("123", "Test") // Exception: 10 digits chahiye!

}

Real-World Example — Android RecyclerView Style

Kotlin
// Yeh pattern Android apps mein use hota hai
class ProductCard(
    val productId: Int,
    val name: String,
    val price: Double,
    val category: String,

val discount: Int = 0, // Default: koi discount nahi

val inStock: Boolean = true // Default: available

) {

// Price calculations — computed in init

val discountedPrice: Double

val savings: Double

init {

require(price > 0) { "Price positive hona chahiye" }

require(discount in 0..100) { "Discount 0-100% ke beech hona chahiye" }

discountedPrice = price * (1 - discount / 100.0)

savings = price - discountedPrice

}

// Secondary constructor — sirf naam aur price se banana

constructor(name: String, price: Double) : this(

productId = (1000..9999).random(),

name = name,

price = price,

category = "General"

)

fun displayCard() {

        println("┌──────────────────────────────┐")
        println("│ 🛍️  $name")
        println("│ Category: $category")

if (discount > 0) {

            println("│ MRP: ₹$price  →  ₹$discountedPrice")
            println("│ 🏷️  ${discount}% OFF! Save ₹$savings")

} else {

            println("│ Price: ₹$price")

}

        println("│ ${if (inStock) "✅ In Stock" else "❌ Out of Stock"}")
        println("└──────────────────────────────┘")

}

}

fun main() {

val product1 = ProductCard(1, "iPhone 15", 79999.0, "Electronics", 10)

product1.displayCard()

val product2 = ProductCard(2, "Earbuds", 1999.0, "Accessories")

product2.displayCard()

// Secondary constructor use

val product3 = ProductCard("Keyboard", 2500.0)

product3.displayCard()

}

⚠️️ Common Mistakes
Kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: init mein heavy kaam karna
class Database(val url: String) {
    init {
        // Thread.sleep(5000)  ← KABHI MAT KARO!
        // Network call ← KABHI MAT KARO!
        // Ye UI freeze kar deta hai Android mein!
    }
}

// ❌ GALTI 2: val ko init mein badalna
class MyClass(val name: String) {
    init {

// name = "New Name" ← ERROR! val hai, change nahi hoga

}

}

// ❌ GALTI 3: Secondary constructor primary ko bhoolna

class Vehicle(val type: String) {

// constructor(type: String, speed: Int) { } ← ERROR!

// Primary ko call karna zaroori!

// ✅ SAHI:

constructor(type: String, speed: Int) : this(type) {

println("Speed: $speed")

}

}

// ❌ GALTI 4: Default values hote hue secondary constructor banana

// class Pizza(val size: String) {

// constructor() : this("Medium") ← Zarurat nahi!

// }

// ✅ SAHI — Default value do!

class Pizza(val size: String = "Medium")

💡Pro Tips
💡TIP 1: Default values use karo instead of multiple secondary constructors

class Config(val host: String = "localhost", val port: Int = 8080)

💡TIP 2: init mein require() se validation karo

require(age >= 0) { "Age negative nahi ho sakta!" }

check(name.isNotBlank()) { "Naam khali nahi ho sakta!" }

💡TIP 3: Constructor parameters ko val/var banao — auto property ban jaati hai

class Dog(val name: String) ← name property automatically bani

class Dog(name: String) ← Sirf constructor parameter, property nahi!

💡TIP 4: Named arguments use karo — code readable hota hai

val p = Person(name = "Rahul", age = 25, city = "Delhi")

🎯 When NOT to Use Secondary Constructor

❌Secondary constructor mat banao jab:

- Default values kaam kar sakti hain

- Logic same hai sirf parameters alag hain

✅Secondary constructor tab banao jab:

- Alag initialization logic chahiye

- Factory pattern implement karna ho

- Existing code compatibility maintain karni ho

🔥 Interview Questions

Q1: Primary aur Secondary Constructor mein kya fark hai?

Primary Constructor:

- Class header mein hota hai

- Sabse simple aur preferred

- val/var se directly property banti hai

Secondary Constructor:

- Class body mein hota hai

- 'constructor' keyword se shuru

- Primary ko this() se call karna zaroori

Real answer: "Kotlin mein primary constructor prefer karo with

default values. Secondary tab jab alag initialization logic ho."

Q2: init block aur constructor mein kya fark hai?

Constructor = Object banane ki request (input lena)

init block = Object banate waqt kya karna hai (processing)

Multiple init blocks ho sakte hain — upar se neeche chaltey hain.

init block primary constructor ke baad chalta hai.

Secondary constructor ke baad bhi init chalta hai.

🧩 Practice Question

Sawaal: Ek Pizza class banao:

Properties: size, crust, toppings, price

Default: crust = "Thin", toppings = "None"

init mein print karo: "Pizza order ho gayi!"

Method: showOrder() jo sab kuch print kare

Kotlin
class Pizza(
    val size: String,
    val crust: String = "Thin",
    val toppings: String = "None",
    val price: Double
) {
    init {
        println("🍕 Pizza order ho gayi!")
        require(price > 0) { "Price positive honi chahiye!" }
    }
    
    fun showOrder() {
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
        println("🍕 Pizza Order")
        println("Size: $size")
        println("Crust: $crust")
        println("Toppings: $toppings")
        println("Price: ₹$price")
        println("━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val p1 = Pizza("Large", price = 499.0)  // Default crust aur toppings
    p1.showOrder()
    
    val p2 = Pizza("Medium", "Thick", "Paneer + Corn", 399.0)
    p2.showOrder()
}
🧠 Quick Summary + Memory Trick
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  QUICK SUMMARY                                         │
│  • Constructor = Object create hote time auto setup   │
│  • Primary = class header mein (preferred!)            │
│  • Secondary = class body mein (constructor keyword)  │
│  • init block = initialization logic                  │
│  • Default values = multiple constructors ki zarurat  │
│    nahi padti                                          │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

🧠 MEMORY TRICK:

"Constructor = HOSPITAL ADMISSION FORM"

Admit hote waqt sab fill karo — baad mein nahi!

Primary form = basic info (zaroori)

Secondary form = extra info (optional)

#3
ENCAPSULATION

🧒 Simple Explanation

Socho tumhara piggy bank hai (gulab jamun box wala, jisme paisa save karte the bachpan mein! 🐷).

PIGGY BANK:

┌─────────────────┐
│  💰 Money       │  ← Andar se dikhta nahi
│  (Hidden!)      │
└────────┬────────┘
         │

Ek hi hole

(Controlled Access!)

Tum seedha andar haath nahi daal sakte.

Sirf ek taraf se paisa daalna allowed hai.

Paisa nikalna = Bank tod do (limited/controlled)

Encapsulation = Data ko chupaao + Controlled access do

🌍 Real-Life Example

ATM Machine = Perfect Encapsulation!

ATM ke andar:

┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
│  💰 Cash Storage          (PRIVATE)   │
│  🔐 Account Database      (PRIVATE)   │
│  ⚙️ Processing System     (PRIVATE)   │
│  🔢 PIN Verification      (PRIVATE)   │
└──────────────────────────────────────-┘

↑

Tumhe andar haath nahi daalna

↓

┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Card Slot        (PUBLIC interface)  │
│  PIN Pad          (PUBLIC interface)  │
│  Amount Buttons   (PUBLIC interface)  │
│  Cash Slot        (PUBLIC interface)  │
└───────────────────────────────────────┘

Tum sirf bahar ke buttons use karte ho.

Internal logic chupaaya hua hai.

🤔 Why Encapsulation Exist Karta Hai?

Kotlin
// BINA ENCAPSULATION KE — Disaster! 🚨

class BankAccount {

var balance = 10000.0 // Public! Koi bhi change kar sakta hai!

}

val acc = BankAccount()

acc.balance = 1000000000.0 // 😱 Koi bhi balance change kar le!

acc.balance = -5000.0 // 😱 Negative balance!

acc.balance = Double.MAX_VALUE // 😱 Kuch bhi kar sakte hain!

// KISI KO BHI ROKA NAHI JA SAKTA!

Kotlin
// ENCAPSULATION KE SAATH — Safe! 🔒

class BankAccount {

private var balance = 10000.0 // Private! Bahar se access nahi

fun deposit(amount: Double) {

if (amount > 0) balance += amount // Validation!

else println("Amount positive honi chahiye!")

}

fun getBalance() = balance // Read-only access

}

val acc = BankAccount()

// acc.balance = 1000000.0 ← ❌ ERROR! Private hai

acc.deposit(-500.0) // "Amount positive honi chahiye!"

acc.deposit(5000.0) // ✅ Balance safe way se badhega

⚙️ Internally Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?

Visibility Modifiers (Darwaze) — 4 levels:

🌐 public = Ghar ka main door

Sab aa sakte hain (Default in Kotlin)

🔒 private = Master bedroom ka lock

Sirf isi class ke andar

👨‍👩‍👧 protected = Family room

Class + uske children (subclasses)

🏢 internal = Office building

Same module/project ke log

CLASS KA STRUCTURE:

┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              BankAccount                   │
│                                            │
│  🔒 PRIVATE ZONE (Gupt/Secret):            │
│     var balance: Double                    │
│     var pin: String                        │
│     fun validatePin(): Boolean             │
│                                            │
│  🌐 PUBLIC ZONE (Bahar diktha hai):        │
│     fun deposit(amount: Double)            │
│     fun withdraw(amount: Double, pin: String│
│     fun getBalance(): Double               │
└────────────────────────────────────────────┘

💻 Kotlin Syntax

Kotlin
class Person(
    val name: String,                  // public (default) — koi bhi dekh sakta

private val aadhaarNumber: String, // private — sirf class ke andar

protected val age: Int, // protected — class + children

internal val department: String // internal — same module

) {

// Private property — custom getter/setter

private var _salary: Double = 0.0

// Public getter — read karo

val salary: Double

get() = _salary

// Public setter with validation

fun setSalary(amount: Double) {

if (amount > 0) _salary = amount

else println("❌ Invalid salary!")

}

}

🧪 Code Examples

Easy Example

Kotlin
class Student {
    // Private — sirf class ke andar change hoga
    private var _marks: Int = 0
    
    // Read-only access — Public
    val marks: Int
        get() = _marks
    
    // Controlled way to set marks
    fun setMarks(value: Int) {
        when {
            value < 0 -> println("❌ Marks negative nahi ho sakte!")
            value > 100 -> println("❌ Marks 100 se zyada nahi ho sakte!")
            else -> {
                _marks = value
                println("✅ Marks set: $_marks")
            }
        }
    }
    
    val grade: String
        get() = when {
            _marks >= 90 -> "A+"
            _marks >= 80 -> "A"
            _marks >= 70 -> "B"
            else -> "C"
        }
}

fun main() {
    val student = Student()
    
    // student._marks = 150  // ❌ ERROR! Private hai
    
    student.setMarks(150)   // ❌ "Marks 100 se zyada nahi ho sakte!"
    student.setMarks(-10)   // ❌ "Marks negative nahi ho sakte!"
    student.setMarks(85)    // ✅ "Marks set: 85"
    
    println("Marks: ${student.marks}")   // ✅ Read kar sakte hain
    println("Grade: ${student.grade}")   // A
}

Medium Example — Full Encapsulation

Kotlin
class MobilePhone(

private val imei: String, // ← Kabhi change nahi hona chahiye

val model: String

) {

private var _batteryLevel: Int = 100

private var _isLocked: Boolean = true

private var _pin: String = "1234"

private val _callLog = mutableListOf<String>()

// Read-only properties

val batteryLevel: Int get() = _batteryLevel

val isLocked: Boolean get() = _isLocked

val callLog: List<String> get() = _callLog.toList() // Copy dete hain, original nahi!

// Unlock karna

fun unlock(pin: String): Boolean {

return if (pin == _pin) {

_isLocked = false

println("🔓 Phone unlock ho gaya!")

true

} else {

println("❌ Galat PIN!")

false

}

}

// Call karna — locked phone se call nahi hoga

fun makeCall(number: String): Boolean {

if (_isLocked) {

println("❌ Phone lock hai! Pehle unlock karo.")

return false

}

_callLog.add(number)

_batteryLevel -= 5

println("📞 Calling $number... Battery: $_batteryLevel%")

return true

}

// PIN change

fun changePin(oldPin: String, newPin: String): Boolean {

if (oldPin != _pin) {

println("❌ Purana PIN galat hai!")

return false

}

if (newPin.length != 4) {

println("❌ PIN 4 digits ka hona chahiye!")

return false

}

_pin = newPin

println("✅ PIN change ho gaya!")

return true

}

fun charge(percentage: Int) {

_batteryLevel = minOf(100, _batteryLevel + percentage)

println("🔋 Battery: $_batteryLevel%")

}

}

fun main() {

val myPhone = MobilePhone("123456789012345", "Samsung S24")

// _imei access nahi hoga — private hai

// myPhone._pin = "0000" ← ERROR!

myPhone.makeCall("9876543210") // ❌ Locked hai

myPhone.unlock("9999") // ❌ Galat PIN

myPhone.unlock("1234") // ✅ Unlock!

myPhone.makeCall("9876543210") // ✅ Call hogi

myPhone.makeCall("1234567890") // ✅ Call hogi

println("Call log: ${myPhone.callLog}")

// myPhone._callLog.clear() ← ❌ ERROR! Private + Copy diya

myPhone.changePin("1234", "5678")

}

Real-World Android Example

// Android mein UserSession manage karna — Encapsulation ka best use

class UserSession {

// Kisi ko bhi seedha access nahi

private var _userId: Int = -1

private var _token: String = ""

private var _username: String = ""

private var _isLoggedIn: Boolean = false

private var _loginTime: Long = 0L

// Read-only public access

val userId: Int get() = _userId

val username: String get() = _username

val isLoggedIn: Boolean get() = _isLoggedIn

// Session kitni der se active hai

val sessionDuration: Long

get() = if (_isLoggedIn) System.currentTimeMillis() - _loginTime else 0L

fun login(userId: Int, username: String, token: String) {

require(token.isNotBlank()) { "Token required!" }

_userId = userId

_username = username

_token = token

_isLoggedIn = true

_loginTime = System.currentTimeMillis()

println("✅ Session started: $username")

}

fun logout() {

_userId = -1

_token = ""

_username = ""

_isLoggedIn = false

_loginTime = 0L

println("👋 Session ended")

}

// Token sirf authorized jagah mile

fun getAuthHeader(): String {

return if (_isLoggedIn) "Bearer $_token"

else throw IllegalStateException("Not logged in!")

}

companion object {

// Singleton — ek hi session

private var instance: UserSession? = null

fun getInstance(): UserSession {

if (instance == null) instance = UserSession()

return instance!!

}

}

}

fun main() {

val session = UserSession.getInstance()

session.login(101, "rahul_dev", "jwt_abc_xyz_123")

println("Logged in: ${session.isLoggedIn}")

println("User: ${session.username}")

println("Auth: ${session.getAuthHeader()}")

// session._token ← ❌ Private!

session.logout()

// session.getAuthHeader() ← ❌ Exception! Not logged in

}

⚠️️ Common Mistakes
Kotlin
// ❌ GALTI 1: Sab kuch public rakhna
class User {

var password: String = "" // ❌ Anyone can read!

var creditCardNumber: String = "" // ❌ Security disaster!

}

// ✅ SAHI

class User {

private var password: String = ""

private var creditCardNumber: String = ""

fun login(inputPassword: String) = password == inputPassword

}

// ❌ GALTI 2: Mutable collection expose karna

class ShoppingCart {

val items = mutableListOf<String>() // ❌ Anyone can clear/modify!

}

val cart = ShoppingCart()

cart.items.clear() // ❌ Seedha modify kar diya!

cart.items.add("Hack") // ❌ Kuch bhi add kar diya!

// ✅ SAHI — Copy return karo

class ShoppingCart {

private val _items = mutableListOf<String>()

val items: List<String> get() = _items.toList() // Immutable copy!

fun addItem(item: String) { _items.add(item) }

fun removeItem(item: String) { _items.remove(item) }

}

// ❌ GALTI 3: Getter/Setter banana instead of Kotlin properties

// Java style — Kotlin mein mat karo!

class Wrong {

private var name: String = ""

fun getName() = name

fun setName(value: String) { name = value }

}

// ✅ Kotlin style — properties!

class Right {

var name: String = ""

private set // Bahar se read-only, andar se change kar sakte hain

}

💡Pro Tips
💡TIP 1: "_" prefix private properties ke liye

private var _items = mutableListOf()

val items: List get() = _items.toList()

💡TIP 2: private set use karo — read public, write private

var score: Int = 0

private set ← Bahar se change nahi hoga!

💡TIP 3: Immutable copy return karo collections ke liye

get() = _list.toList() ← Original safe!

💡TIP 4: require() aur check() use karo validation ke liye

require(amount > 0) { "Positive amount chahiye" }

🔥 Interview Questions

Q1: Encapsulation kya hai aur kyun zaroori hai?

"Encapsulation matlab hai data ko private rakhna aur

sirf controlled methods se access dena.

Kyun zaroori:

1Data protection — koi bhi galat value set na kar sake

2Validation — data set hone se pehle check kar sako

3Flexibility — internal implementation change karo

bina outside code todhe

4Security — sensitive info hide karo

Real example: ATM mein aap seedha cash storage access

nahi karte — sirf interface (buttons) use karte ho."

Q2: private set ka kya matlab hai Kotlin mein?

var count: Int = 0

private set

count ek public property hai — koi bhi read kar sakta hai.

Lekin set (write/change) sirf class ke andar ho sakta hai.

Bahar se: println(obj.count) ← ✅ Read kar sakte

obj.count = 5 ← ❌ Error! Private set

🧩 Practice Question

Sawaal: Ek Wallet class banao:

Private balance

addMoney() — sirf positive amount

spendMoney() — balance check karo pehle

getBalance() — read-only

Kotlin
class Wallet(private var balance: Double = 0.0) {
    
    init {
        require(balance >= 0) { "Balance negative nahi ho sakta!" }
        println("👛 Wallet khola! Balance: ₹$balance")
    }
    
    fun addMoney(amount: Double) {
        if (amount <= 0) {
            println("❌ Amount positive honi chahiye!")
            return
        }
        balance += amount
        println("💰 ₹$amount add hua! Balance: ₹$balance")
    }
    
    fun spendMoney(amount: Double, description: String): Boolean {
        return when {
            amount <= 0 -> {
                println("❌ Amount positive honi chahiye!")
                false
            }
            amount > balance -> {
                println("❌ Paisa kam hai! Balance: ₹$balance, Chahiye: ₹$amount")
                false
            }
            else -> {
                balance -= amount
                println("✅ ₹$amount kharch: $description | Remaining: ₹$balance")
                true
            }
        }
    }
    
    fun getBalance(): Double = balance
}

fun main() {
    val myWallet = Wallet(500.0)
    myWallet.addMoney(1000.0)
    myWallet.spendMoney(200.0, "Chai aur Samosa ☕")
    myWallet.spendMoney(2000.0, "iPhone")  // ❌ Balance kam hai
    myWallet.spendMoney(-50.0, "Hack try")  // ❌ Negative amount
    println("Final balance: ₹${myWallet.getBalance()}")
}
🧠 Quick Summary + Memory Trick
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  QUICK SUMMARY                                       │
│  • Encapsulation = Data chupaao + Safe access do    │
│  • private = Class ke andar hi accessible           │
│  • Public methods = Controlled door/gate            │
│  • Custom getter/setter = Data pe control           │
│  • Immutable copies return karo collections ke liye │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

🧠 MEMORY TRICK:

"Encapsulation = CAPSULE (medicine)"

Capsule ke andar medicine chupi hai.

Tum seedha medicine nahi khaate —

capsule ke through khaate ho (controlled!)

🟦 LEVEL 2: THE 4 PILLARS

#4
INHERITANCE

🧒 Simple Explanation

Socho Papa se tumhe kya mila (inheritance!):

PAPA (Parent):

✅Aankhon ka color — Tumhe mila!
✅Height — Tumhe mila!
✅Nose shape — Tumhe mila!
✅Kuch habits — Tumhe mili!

TUM (Child):

✅Papa wali sab cheezein (inherited)
✅Plus apni nai cheezein (extra skills, personality)

Programming mein:

Parent Class = Papa ki properties aur methods

Child Class = Papa ki cheezein + apni nai cheezein

🌍 Real-Life Example

School mein departments socho:

Employee (Parent Class)

├── name
├── salary
├── workHours()
├── getPayslip()
│
├── Teacher (Child Class)
│   └── + subject, classRoom
│   └── + teachStudents(), gradePapers()
│
├── Principal (Child Class)
│   └── + schoolBudget, yearsOfExperience
│   └── + attendMeetings(), makeDecisions()
│
└── Librarian (Child Class)
    └── + totalBooks, sections
    └── + issueBook(), collectBook()

Sab employees hain — sab ke paas naam, salary hai.

Lekin har ek ka extra kaam alag hai!

🤔 Why Inheritance Exist Karta Hai?

Problem without inheritance:

Kotlin
class Dog {
    var name = ""
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // Same!
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // Same!
    fun bark() = println("$name: Woof!")                // Dog ka apna
}

class Cat {
    var name = ""
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // COPY-PASTE! 😫
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // COPY-PASTE! 😫
    fun meow() = println("$name: Meow!")                // Cat ka apna
}

class Bird {
    var name = ""
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // COPY-PASTE AGAIN! 😩
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // COPY-PASTE AGAIN! 😩
    fun fly() = println("$name ud raha hai")            // Bird ka apna
}
With inheritance:
Kotlin
open class Animal(val name: String) {
    fun eat() = println("$name khaana kha raha hai")  // ← SIRF EK BAAR!
    fun sleep() = println("$name so raha hai")          // ← SIRF EK BAAR!
}

class Dog(name: String) : Animal(name) { fun bark() = println("$name: Woof!") }
class Cat(name: String) : Animal(name) { fun meow() = println("$name: Meow!") }
class Bird(name: String) : Animal(name) { fun fly() = println("$name ud raha hai") }
⚙️ Internally Kaise Kaam Karta Hai?
Dog object ke andar actually yeh hota hai:
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Dog Object               │
│                                    │
│  📦 Animal part (inherited):       │
│     name: String                   │
│     fun eat()                      │
│     fun sleep()                    │
│                                    │
│  📦 Dog's own part:                │
│     fun bark()                     │
│     fun fetch()                    │
└────────────────────────────────────┘

Dog object mein DONO cheezein hain!

Kotlin mein open keyword:

DEFAULT: Every Kotlin class is FINAL (inherit nahi kar sakte)

↓

Kyun? "Jaan boojhkar inheritance allow karo" philosophy

↓

open likhne se: "Haan, is class ko inherit karo!"

💻 Kotlin Syntax

Kotlin
// Parent class — 'open' keyword zaroori!
open class ParentClass(val property: String) {
    
    open fun method() {    // 'open' = override kar sakte ho
        println("Parent method")
    }
    
    fun finalMethod() {   // 'open' nahi = override NAHI kar sakte
        println("This cannot be overridden!")
    }
}

// Child class — ':' se inherit karo
class ChildClass(property: String) : ParentClass(property) {
    
    override fun method() {  // 'override' zaroori!
        super.method()       // Parent ka method bhi chalaao (optional)
        println("Child method")
    }
    
    fun extraMethod() {      // Child ka apna naya method
        println("Only in Child")
    }
}
🧪 Code Examples

Easy Example

Kotlin
// Parent class
open class Animal(val name: String, val sound: String) {
    
    // Yeh method sabko milega
    fun eat() {
        println("$name khaana kha raha hai 🍽️")
    }
    
    // Yeh override ho sakta hai
    open fun makeSound() {
        println("$name bol raha hai: $sound")
    }
    
    fun sleep() {
        println("$name so raha hai 😴")
    }
}

// Child classes
class Dog(name: String) : Animal(name, "Woof!") {
    
    override fun makeSound() {
        println("$name bhaunka: WOOF WOOF! 🐕")  // Override!
    }
    
    fun fetch() {
        println("$name ball laa raha hai! 🎾")
    }
}

class Cat(name: String) : Animal(name, "Meow!") {
    
    override fun makeSound() {
        println("$name bol raha hai: Meow~ 🐈")
    }
    
    fun purr() {
        println("$name purr kar raha hai... 😻")
    }
}

class Bird(name: String) : Animal(name, "Tweet!") {
    // makeSound() override nahi kiya — parent ka chalega!
    
    fun fly() {
        println("$name ud raha hai! 🐦")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val dog = Dog("Bruno")
    dog.eat()          // ← Animal se inherited! "Bruno khaana kha raha hai"
    dog.makeSound()    // ← Override! "Bruno bhaunka: WOOF WOOF!"
    dog.fetch()        // ← Dog ka apna! "Bruno ball laa raha hai!"
    dog.sleep()        // ← Animal se inherited!
    
    println()
    
    val bird = Bird("Tweety")
    bird.eat()         // Animal se
    bird.makeSound()   // Parent ka chalega: "Tweety bol raha hai: Tweet!"
    bird.fly()         // Bird ka apna
}

Medium Example — Multi-Level Inheritance

Kotlin
// Level 1 — Sabse upar
open class Vehicle(val brand: String, val speed: Int) {
    open fun move() = println("$brand chal raha hai!")
    open fun stop() = println("$brand ruk gaya!")
    fun fuelType() = println("Fuel type define nahi hua")
}

// Level 2 — Vehicle se inherit kiya
open class Car(brand: String, speed: Int, val doors: Int) : Vehicle(brand, speed) {
    override fun move() {
        super.move()  // Parent ka bhi chalate hain
        println("  ↳ Car $doors doors ke saath!")
    }
    
    open fun honk() = println("$brand: Beep! Beep! 📯")
}

// Level 3 — Car se inherit kiya
