SECTION 3
ADVANCED OOP IN KOTLIN

1📊 DATA CLASSES

What is it?

Data class ek special class hai jo primarily data hold karne ke liye hoti hai. Kotlin automatically equals(), hashCode(), toString(), copy(), aur componentN() functions generate karta hai.

Why use it?

Kotlin
// Normal class — 40+ lines likhne padte hain:
class User(val name: String, val email: String) {
    override fun equals(other: Any?): Boolean { /* boilerplate */ }
    override fun hashCode(): Int { /* boilerplate */ }
    override fun toString(): String { /* boilerplate */ }
    fun copy(name: String = this.name, email: String = this.email) = User(name, email)
}

// Data class — 1 line!
data class User(val name: String, val email: String)

// Kotlin generates ALL of the above automatically!

Syntax & Features:

Kotlin
data class Product(
    val id: Int,
    val name: String,
    val price: Double,

val category: String = "General" // Default values allowed!

) {

// Extra properties NOT in constructor (not part of equals/hashCode)

val formattedPrice: String

get() = "₹$price"

// Extra methods allowed!

fun applyDiscount(percent: Double) = copy(price = price * (1 - percent / 100))

}

fun main() {

val p1 = Product(1, "Phone", 15000.0, "Electronics")

val p2 = Product(1, "Phone", 15000.0, "Electronics")

val p3 = Product(2, "Laptop", 55000.0)

// toString() — auto generated!

println(p1) // Product(id=1, name=Phone, price=15000.0, category=Electronics)

// equals() — structural equality!

println(p1 == p2) // true (same data)

println(p1 == p3) // false

// copy() — immutable update pattern!

val updated = p1.copy(price = 12000.0)

println(updated) // Product(id=1, name=Phone, price=12000.0, category=Electronics)

// Discount apply

val discounted = p1.applyDiscount(10.0)

println(discounted) // price = 13500.0

// Destructuring (componentN functions)

val (id, name, price, category) = p1

println("$id: $name costs ₹$price in $category")

// In a Set (uses hashCode)

val products = setOf(p1, p2, p3) // p1 and p2 are same, so only 2 in set

println("Unique products: ${products.size}") // 2

}

Real-World API Response Model:

Kotlin
data class ApiResponse<T>(
    val success: Boolean,
    val data: T? = null,
    val error: String? = null,
    val statusCode: Int = 200
) {
    val isSuccess: Boolean get() = success && error == null
}

data class LoginRequest(
    val username: String,
    val password: String,
    val rememberMe: Boolean = false
)

data class UserDTO(
    val id: Long,
    val username: String,
    val email: String,
    val role: String,
    val token: String
)

fun simulateLogin(request: LoginRequest): ApiResponse<UserDTO> {
    return if (request.username == "admin" && request.password == "admin123") {
        ApiResponse(
            success = true,
            data = UserDTO(1L, "admin", "admin@app.com", "ADMIN", "jwt_token_xyz"),
            statusCode = 200
        )
    } else {
        ApiResponse(
            success = false,
            error = "Invalid credentials",
            statusCode = 401
        )
    }
}

fun main() {
    val request = LoginRequest("admin", "admin123", true)
    val response = simulateLogin(request)
    
    if (response.isSuccess) {
        val user = response.data!!
        println("Welcome ${user.username}! Token: ${user.token}")
    } else {
        println("Error ${response.statusCode}: ${response.error}")
    }
    
    // Copy for testing
    val wrongRequest = request.copy(password = "wrongpass")
    val failResponse = simulateLogin(wrongRequest)
    println("Failed login: ${failResponse.error}")
}
2. 🔒 SEALED CLASSES

What is it?

Sealed class ek restricted class hierarchy hai — uske subclasses limited aur defined hoti hain. Compiler ko pata hota hai ki sealed class ke exactly kaun kaun se subtypes hain.

Sealed Class = "Closed world" — sab possibilities known hain

Like traffic light: only RED, YELLOW, GREEN — no other option

Why use it?

when expression ke saath perfect — else branch ki zarurat nahi kyunki compiler jaanta hai sab cases!

Syntax:

Kotlin
sealed class Result<out T> {
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : Result<T>()
    data class Error(val message: String, val code: Int = -1) : Result<Nothing>()
    object Loading : Result<Nothing>()
    object Empty : Result<Nothing>()
}

// Usage — EXHAUSTIVE when (no else needed!)
fun handleResult(result: Result<String>) = when (result) {
    is Result.Success -> println("✅ Data: ${result.data}")
    is Result.Error -> println("❌ Error ${result.code}: ${result.message}")
    is Result.Loading -> println("⏳ Loading...")
    is Result.Empty -> println("📭 No data")
    // No else needed — all cases covered!
}

Real-World Example — UI States:

Kotlin
sealed class UiState<out T> {
    object Idle : UiState<Nothing>()
    object Loading : UiState<Nothing>()
    data class Success<T>(val data: T) : UiState<T>()
    data class Error(
        val message: String,
        val throwable: Throwable? = null,
        val isRetryable: Boolean = true
    ) : UiState<Nothing>()
}

sealed class NavigationEvent {
    object Back : NavigationEvent()
    data class ToDetail(val id: Long) : NavigationEvent()
    data class ToProfile(val userId: Int) : NavigationEvent()
    data class ShowDialog(val title: String, val message: String) : NavigationEvent()
    object ToLogin : NavigationEvent()
}

class ProductViewModel {

private var uiState: UiState<List<String>> = UiState.Idle

fun loadProducts() {

uiState = UiState.Loading

renderUi()

// Simulate network delay

Thread.sleep(100)

// Simulate success

uiState = UiState.Success(listOf("Phone", "Laptop", "Tablet"))

renderUi()

}

fun loadFailed() {

uiState = UiState.Error("Network error", isRetryable = true)

renderUi()

}

private fun renderUi() = when (val state = uiState) {

is UiState.Idle -> println("🔵 Idle state")

is UiState.Loading -> println("⏳ Showing loading spinner...")

is UiState.Success -> println("✅ Showing ${state.data.size} products: ${state.data}")

is UiState.Error -> {

println("❌ Error: ${state.message}")

if (state.isRetryable) println(" [Retry] button shown")

}

}

}

fun handleNavigation(event: NavigationEvent) = when (event) {

is NavigationEvent.Back -> println("⬅️ Going back")

is NavigationEvent.ToDetail -> println("📱 Opening detail: ${event.id}")

is NavigationEvent.ToProfile -> println("👤 Opening profile: ${event.userId}")

is NavigationEvent.ShowDialog -> println("💬 Dialog: ${event.title} — ${event.message}")

is NavigationEvent.ToLogin -> println("🔐 Going to login")

}

fun main() {

val vm = ProductViewModel()

vm.loadProducts()

vm.loadFailed()

handleNavigation(NavigationEvent.ToDetail(42L))

handleNavigation(NavigationEvent.ShowDialog("Error", "Please try again"))

}

3🎨 ENUM CLASSES

What is it?

Enum class ek fixed set of constants define karta hai. Jab possible values limited aur known hain.

Kotlin
enum class Direction { NORTH, SOUTH, EAST, WEST }
enum class DayOfWeek { MON, TUE, WED, THU, FRI, SAT, SUN }

Advanced Enum with Properties & Methods:

Kotlin
enum class HttpStatus(val code: Int, val description: String) {
    OK(200, "Success"),
    CREATED(201, "Created"),
    BAD_REQUEST(400, "Bad Request"),
    UNAUTHORIZED(401, "Unauthorized"),
    NOT_FOUND(404, "Not Found"),
    INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR(500, "Internal Server Error");
    
    val isSuccess: Boolean get() = code in 200..299
    val isClientError: Boolean get() = code in 400..499
    val isServerError: Boolean get() = code in 500..599
    
    fun toResponse(): String = "$code $description"
    
    companion object {
        fun fromCode(code: Int): HttpStatus? = values().find { it.code == code }
    }
}

enum class Priority(val level: Int, val label: String) {
    LOW(1, "🟢 Low"),
    MEDIUM(5, "🟡 Medium"),
    HIGH(8, "🔴 High"),
    CRITICAL(10, "🚨 Critical");
    
    fun isUrgent() = level >= HIGH.level
    
    companion object {
        fun fromLevel(level: Int) = values().minByOrNull { Math.abs(it.level - level) }!!
    }
}

fun main() {
    // Basic usage
    val status = HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND
    println(status.toResponse())      // 404 Not Found
    println(status.isClientError)     // true
    
    // Find by code
    val found = HttpStatus.fromCode(500)
    println(found?.description)       // Internal Server Error
    
    // Enum in when
    val response = HttpStatus.OK
    val message = when (response) {
        HttpStatus.OK, HttpStatus.CREATED -> "Request successful!"
        HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST -> "Check your input"
        HttpStatus.UNAUTHORIZED -> "Please login"
        HttpStatus.NOT_FOUND -> "Resource not found"
        HttpStatus.INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR -> "Server error, try later"
    }
    println(message)
    
    // Enum properties
    println(Priority.CRITICAL.isUrgent())  // true
    println(Priority.LOW.isUrgent())       // false
    
    // Iteration
    Priority.values().forEach { p ->
        println("${p.label}: Level ${p.level} | Urgent: ${p.isUrgent()}")
    }
}
4. 🤝 COMPANION OBJECTS

What is it?

Companion Object ek singleton object hai jo kisi class ke saath associated hota hai. Java ke static members ki tarah kaam karta hai.

Kotlin
class MyClass {
    companion object {
        // Accessed as: MyClass.METHOD_NAME (like static)
        const val MAX_SIZE = 100
        fun create() = MyClass()
    }
}

Examples:

Kotlin
class DatabaseConfig private constructor(
    val host: String,
    val port: Int,
    val database: String
) {
    companion object {
        private const val DEFAULT_HOST = "localhost"
        private const val DEFAULT_PORT = 5432
        
        // Factory methods
        fun development() = DatabaseConfig(DEFAULT_HOST, DEFAULT_PORT, "dev_db")
        fun production() = DatabaseConfig("prod.server.com", DEFAULT_PORT, "prod_db")
        fun test() = DatabaseConfig(DEFAULT_HOST, 5433, "test_db")
        
        // Utility
        fun fromUrl(url: String): DatabaseConfig {
            // Parse: "host:port/database"
            val parts = url.split(":", "/")
            return DatabaseConfig(parts[0], parts[1].toInt(), parts[2])
        }
    }
    
    override fun toString() = "DatabaseConfig($host:$port/$database)"
}

fun main() {
    val devDb = DatabaseConfig.development()  // Like static method!
    val prodDb = DatabaseConfig.production()
    val fromUrl = DatabaseConfig.fromUrl("myserver:3306/mydb")
    
    println(devDb)    // DatabaseConfig(localhost:5432/dev_db)
    println(prodDb)
    println(fromUrl)
    
    // Private constructor — can't do new DatabaseConfig(...)
    // Only factory methods work!
}

What is it?

object keyword se ek singleton class create hoti hai — ek hi instance kabhi bhi. Automatically thread-safe in Kotlin.

Kotlin
object AppConfig {

}

// Usage — no instantiation needed!
println(AppConfig.APP_NAME)
AppConfig.debugMode = true
println(AppConfig.getBaseUrl())

// One-time use implementation
val clickListener = object : OnItemClickListener {

        println("Clicked position $position")
    }
}
6. 🔄 DELEGATION

What is it?

Delegation = "Kaam kisi aur ko de do." by keyword se Kotlin automatically delegation implement karta hai.

Two Types:

Class Delegation:

Kotlin
interface Logger {
    fun log(message: String)
    fun logError(message: String)
}

class ConsoleLogger : Logger {
    override fun log(message: String) = println("ℹ️ $message")
    override fun logError(message: String) = println("❌ ERROR: $message")
}

class FileLogger : Logger {
    override fun log(message: String) = println("📁 [FILE] $message")
    override fun logError(message: String) = println("📁 [FILE ERROR] $message")
}

// UserService delegates logging to a Logger implementation

class UserService(

private val logger: Logger = ConsoleLogger()

) : Logger by logger { // ← DELEGATION! All Logger methods forwarded to logger

fun createUser(name: String) {

log("Creating user: $name") // Delegates to logger.log()

// ... create user logic

log("User $name created successfully")

}

fun deleteUser(id: Int) {

if (id <= 0) {

logError("Invalid user ID: $id") // Delegates to logger.logError()

return

}

log("Deleting user $id")

}

}

fun main() {

val service = UserService(ConsoleLogger())

service.createUser("Rahul")

service.deleteUser(-1)

// Switch to file logger

val fileService = UserService(FileLogger())

fileService.createUser("Priya")

}

Property Delegation:

Kotlin
import kotlin.properties.Delegates

class ObservableViewModel {
    // Observable — notifies on change
    var username: String by Delegates.observable("") { property, oldValue, newValue ->
        println("${property.name} changed: '$oldValue' → '$newValue'")
        validateUsername(newValue)
    }
    
    var age: Int by Delegates.vetoable(0) { property, oldValue, newValue ->
        // Veto — reject if condition not met
        val accepted = newValue in 0..120
        if (!accepted) println("❌ Invalid age: $newValue (rejected!)")

accepted // return true to accept, false to reject

}

private fun validateUsername(name: String) {

if (name.length < 3) println("⚠️ Username too short!")

}

}

fun main() {

val vm = ObservableViewModel()

vm.username = "Ra" // username changed: '' → 'Ra' | Too short!

vm.username = "Rahul" // username changed: 'Ra' → 'Rahul'

vm.age = 25 // accepted

vm.age = 150 // rejected! stays 25

vm.age = -5 // rejected! stays 25

println("Final age: ${vm.age}") // 25

}

7🔧 EXTENSION FUNCTIONS

What is it?

Extension functions existing classes mein naye methods add karne ka tarika — bina subclassing ke!

Jaise tum ek existing phone mein new app install karo bina hardware change kiye

Syntax & Examples:

Kotlin
// String pe extension function
fun String.isPalindrome(): Boolean {
    return this == this.reversed()
}

fun String.toTitleCase(): String {
    return split(" ").joinToString(" ") { word ->
        word.lowercase().replaceFirstChar { it.uppercase() }
    }
}

fun String.truncate(maxLength: Int, suffix: String = "..."): String {
    return if (length <= maxLength) this else take(maxLength - suffix.length) + suffix
}

// Int pe extension
fun Int.times(block: () -> Unit) {
    repeat(this) { block() }
}

fun Double.toRupees(): String = "₹${"%.2f".format(this)}"

// List pe extension
fun <T> List<T>.second(): T {
    if (size < 2) throw NoSuchElementException("List has less than 2 elements")
    return this[1]
}

fun List<Int>.median(): Double {
    val sorted = sorted()
    val mid = size / 2
    return if (size % 2 == 0) (sorted[mid - 1] + sorted[mid]) / 2.0 else sorted[mid].toDouble()
}

// Extension on custom class
data class User(val name: String, val email: String, val age: Int)

fun User.isAdult() = age >= 18
fun User.toDisplayString() = "$name (${email}) - Age: $age"
fun List<User>.adults() = filter { it.isAdult() }
fun List<User>.averageAge() = if (isEmpty()) 0.0 else map { it.age }.average()

fun main() {
    println("madam".isPalindrome())           // true
    println("hello world".toTitleCase())      // Hello World
    println("This is a very long text".truncate(15))  // This is a ve...
    
    3.times { print("Ha! ") }  // Ha! Ha! Ha!
    println()
    
    println(15000.0.toRupees())   // ₹15000.00
    
    val nums = listOf(3, 1, 4, 1, 5, 9, 2, 6)
    println(nums.median())        // 3.5
    
    val users = listOf(
        User("Rahul", "r@x.com", 25),
        User("Little Priya", "l@x.com", 15),
        User("Arjun", "a@x.com", 30)
    )
    println(users.adults().map { it.name })  // [Rahul, Arjun]
    println(users.averageAge())               // ~23.3
    println(users[0].toDisplayString())
}
8. ⏰ LATEINIT VS LAZY
lateinit — Baad mein initialize karo:
Kotlin
class MyActivity {

lateinit var userProfile: UserProfile // Non-null, initialized later

lateinit var database: Database

fun initialize() {

userProfile = UserProfile("Rahul", "rahul@test.com")

database = Database("db_url")

}

fun showProfile() {

// Check before use to avoid UninitializedPropertyAccessException

if (::userProfile.isInitialized) {

println(userProfile)

} else {

println("Profile not initialized!")

}

}

}

lazy — Pehli baar use hone pe initialize karo:

Kotlin
class AppDataManager {
    // Only initialized when first accessed!
    val expensiveConfig: Map<String, String> by lazy {
        println("Initializing config (heavy operation)...")
        mapOf("api_key" to "abc123", "timeout" to "30000", "retry" to "3")
    }
    
    val networkClient by lazy {
        println("Creating network client...")
        NetworkClient("https://api.app.com")
    }
    
    // Lazy is thread-safe by default (LazyThreadSafetyMode.SYNCHRONIZED)
    val analyticsEngine by lazy(LazyThreadSafetyMode.PUBLICATION) {
        AnalyticsEngine()
    }
}

class NetworkClient(val baseUrl: String) {
    fun get(path: String) = println("GET $baseUrl$path")
}
class AnalyticsEngine {
    fun track(event: String) = println("Tracking: $event")
}

fun main() {
    val manager = AppDataManager()
    println("Manager created — no initialization yet")
    
    // First access — initializes!
    println("API Key: ${manager.expensiveConfig["api_key"]}")
    // Second access — already initialized, no re-init
    println("Timeout: ${manager.expensiveConfig["timeout"]}")
    
    manager.networkClient.get("/users")
}
9. 📍 THIS KEYWORD
Kotlin
class Person(val name: String) {
    var age: Int = 0
    
    // Disambiguate parameter vs property
    fun setAge(age: Int) {

this.age = age // this.age = property, age = parameter

}

// Return current instance (Builder pattern)

fun withAge(age: Int): Person {

this.age = age

return this // Return self!

}

// Pass self as argument

fun register(registry: PersonRegistry) {

registry.add(this) // Pass current object

}

// In init

init {

println("Creating ${this.name}") // this is optional here

}

}

class PersonRegistry {

private val people = mutableListOf<Person>()

fun add(person: Person) { people.add(person); println("Registered: ${person.name}") }

fun count() = people.size

}

// Builder pattern with this

fun main() {

val p = Person("Rahul")

p.setAge(25)

// Method chaining with this

val p2 = Person("Priya").withAge(22)

val registry = PersonRegistry()

p.register(registry)

p2.register(registry)

println("Total: ${registry.count()}")

}

10⬆️ SUPER KEYWORD

Kotlin
open class Animal(val name: String) {
    open fun describe(): String = "I am $name, an animal"
    open fun makeSound(): String = "..."
    
    fun breathe() = println("$name is breathing")
}

open class Mammal(name: String, val furColor: String) : Animal(name) {
    override fun describe(): String {
        // Call parent first, then add own info
        return "${super.describe()}, a mammal with $furColor fur"
    }
    
    override fun makeSound() = "Generic mammal sound"
}

class Dog(name: String, furColor: String, val breed: String) : Mammal(name, furColor) {
    override fun describe(): String {
        // Multi-level super chaining
        return "${super.describe()}, specifically a $breed dog"
    }
    
    override fun makeSound() = "Woof!"
    
    fun fullInfo() {
        println(describe())
        println("Sound: ${makeSound()}")
        super.breathe()  // Call grandparent's method (if not overridden in parent)
    }
}

fun main() {
    val dog = Dog("Bruno", "Golden", "Labrador")
    dog.fullInfo()

// I am Bruno, an animal, a mammal with Golden fur, specifically a Labrador dog

// Sound: Woof!

// Bruno is breathing

}

11🏠 INNER VS NESTED CLASSES

Kotlin
class Outer(val outerProp: String) {
    
    private val outerPrivate = "secret"

// NESTED CLASS — static-like, NO access to outer members

class Nested {

fun show() {

// outerProp // ❌ Cannot access outer members!

println("I am a nested class — independent")

}

}

// INNER CLASS — has access to outer members

inner class Inner {

fun show() {

println("Outer prop: $outerProp") // ✅ Can access!

println("Outer private: $outerPrivate") // ✅ Even private!

println("I am inner class")

}

}

}

fun main() {

// Nested — no outer instance needed

val nested = Outer.Nested()

nested.show()

// Inner — NEEDS outer instance

val outer = Outer("Hello")

val inner = outer.Inner()

inner.show()

}

Real-World Inner Class Example:

Kotlin
class RecyclerViewAdapter(private val items: List<String>) {
    
    private var selectedPosition = -1

// Inner ViewHolder — needs access to adapter state

inner class ViewHolder(val position: Int) {

fun bind() {

val item = items[position] // ✅ Accessing outer's items

println("Binding: $item at position $position")

if (position == selectedPosition) {

println(" ★ SELECTED")

}

}

fun onClick() {

selectedPosition = position // ✅ Modifying outer's state

println("Selected position: $selectedPosition")

}

}

fun createViewHolder(position: Int) = ViewHolder(position)

fun getItemCount() = items.size

}

fun main() {

val adapter = RecyclerViewAdapter(listOf("Apple", "Banana", "Cherry"))

val vh1 = adapter.createViewHolder(0)

val vh2 = adapter.createViewHolder(1)

vh1.bind()

vh1.onClick() // Select position 0

vh1.bind() // Shows ★ SELECTED

vh2.bind()

}

SECTION 4
COMPARISONS

1Abstract Class vs Interface

FeatureAbstract ClassInterface
Instantiation❌ Cannot❌ Cannot
Constructor✅ Yes❌ No
Multiple inheritance❌ One only✅ Multiple
State (stored properties)✅ Yes❌ No (only abstract)
Default methods✅ Yes✅ Yes (Kotlin)
Access modifiersAllPublic by default
When to use"IS-A" + shared state"CAN-DO" behavior contract
Kotlin
// Abstract class — HAS-A relationship + state
abstract class Animal(val name: String) {  // Constructor + state
    abstract fun sound(): String
    fun eat() = println("$name eating")  // Shared implementation
}

// Interface — CAN-DO capability
interface Swimmable { fun swim() }
interface Flyable { fun fly() }

class Duck(name: String) : Animal(name), Swimmable, Flyable {
    override fun sound() = "Quack!"
    override fun swim() = println("$name swimming")
    override fun fly() = println("$name flying")
}
// Duck IS-AN Animal, CAN-SWIM, CAN-FLY
Rule of Thumb:

Abstract Class: "It IS a type of..." (Dog IS-AN Animal)
Interface: "It CAN do..." (Duck CAN-FLY, CAN-SWIM)
2. Data Class vs Normal Class
Feature	Data Class	Normal Class
equals()	Auto (structural)	Reference equality
hashCode()	Auto	Not generated
toString()	Auto (all props)	Default (address)
copy()	Auto	Manual
componentN()	Auto (destructuring)	No
InheritanceCannot be openCan be open
Use casePure data holdingBusiness logic
Kotlin
data class Point(val x: Int, val y: Int)

val p1 = Point(1, 2)
val p2 = Point(1, 2)
println(p1 == p2)    // true (structural equality!)
println(p1)          // Point(x=1, y=2)
val p3 = p1.copy(y = 10)  // Point(x=1, y=10)
val (x, y) = p1      // Destructuring!

class Point2(val x: Int, val y: Int)
val q1 = Point2(1, 2)
val q2 = Point2(1, 2)
println(q1 == q2)    // false (reference equality!)
println(q1)          // Point2@hexaddress
3. Sealed vs Enum
Feature	Sealed Class	Enum Class
State per instance✅ Different per instance❌ Fixed per type
Hierarchy✅ Can have subclasses❌ All same level
Data carrying✅ Each subclass can differLimited
InstancesMultiple per typeOne per constant
Pattern matching✅ Perfect with when✅ Good with when
Kotlin
// Enum — fixed constants, each one same structure
enum class Color { RED, GREEN, BLUE }

// Sealed — each subtype can have different data!
sealed class Event {
    data class UserClicked(val x: Int, val y: Int) : Event()  // Has coords
    data class KeyPressed(val keyCode: Int, val char: Char) : Event()  // Has key info
    data class NetworkError(val code: Int, val message: String) : Event()  // Has error
    object AppStarted : Event()  // No data
}

fun handleEvent(event: Event) = when (event) {
    is Event.UserClicked -> println("Clicked at (${event.x}, ${event.y})")
    is Event.KeyPressed -> println("Key: ${event.char}")
    is Event.NetworkError -> println("Error ${event.code}: ${event.message}")
    is Event.AppStarted -> println("App started!")
}
4. Object vs Companion Object
Feature	Object	Companion Object
Associated with class❌ Standalone✅ Tied to a class
AccessObjectName.method()ClassName.method()
Can implement interfaces✅ Yes✅ Yes
Singleton✅ Yes✅ Yes (per class)
Primary useGlobal singletonStatic-like members
Kotlin
// Object — standalone singleton
object NetworkManager {
    fun get(url: String) = println("GET $url")
}
NetworkManager.get("/api/users")

// Companion Object — class-level "static" members
class User private constructor(val name: String) {
    companion object {
        fun create(name: String) = User(name)  // Factory
        const val MAX_NAME_LENGTH = 50
    }
}
val user = User.create("Rahul")  // Class name access
5. Lateinit vs Lazy
Feature	lateinit	lazy
var or val	var only	val only
Type	Non-null types only	Any type
When initManually, any timeFirst access
Thread safetyNot by defaultBy default
Primitives❌ No✅ Yes
Check initialized::prop.isInitializedAlways initialized on access
Kotlin
class Demo {
    lateinit var userService: UserService   // Inject later (DI pattern)
    val config: Map<String, String> by lazy {  // Load once on first access
        loadConfig()
    }
    
    private fun loadConfig() = mapOf("key" to "value")
}
// lateinit = "I will set this later, I promise"

// lazy = "Don't set until needed, then remember it"

SECTION 5
REAL-WORLD APPLICATION

🚗 Vehicle Management System — Complete OOP Project

Kotlin
// ============================================================
// VEHICLE MANAGEMENT SYSTEM

// Demonstrates: Classes, Inheritance, Interfaces, Abstraction,

// Sealed Classes, Data Classes, Companion Objects, Extension Functions

// ============================================================

// --- INTERFACES ---

interface Trackable {

val vehicleId: String

fun getCurrentLocation(): String

fun updateLocation(location: String)

}

interface Insurable {

val insuranceNumber: String

val expiryDate: String

fun isInsuranceValid(): Boolean

fun renewInsurance(newExpiry: String)

}

interface Serviceable {

fun getServiceHistory(): List<ServiceRecord>

fun addServiceRecord(record: ServiceRecord)

fun isDueForService(): Boolean

}

// --- DATA CLASSES ---

data class ServiceRecord(

val date: String,

val type: String,

val cost: Double,

val description: String,

val odometer: Int

)

data class FuelRecord(

val date: String,

val liters: Double,

val costPerLiter: Double,

val odometer: Int

) {

val totalCost: Double get() = liters * costPerLiter

}

data class Owner(

val id: String,

val name: String,

val phone: String,

val email: String

)

// --- SEALED CLASS for Vehicle State ---

sealed class VehicleStatus {

object Parked : VehicleStatus()

data class Moving(val speed: Int, val direction: String) : VehicleStatus()

data class InService(val serviceType: String, val estimatedHours: Int) : VehicleStatus()

data class Broken(val issue: String, val severity: String) : VehicleStatus()

}

// --- ENUM ---

enum class FuelType(val label: String, val co2PerLiter: Double) {

PETROL("Petrol ⛽", 2.31),

DIESEL("Diesel 🛢️", 2.68),

CNG("CNG 💨", 1.8),

ELECTRIC("Electric ⚡", 0.0),

HYBRID("Hybrid 🔋", 1.2);

val isEcoFriendly: Boolean get() = co2PerLiter < 2.0

}

// --- ABSTRACT BASE CLASS ---

abstract class Vehicle(

val brand: String,

val model: String,

val year: Int,

val fuelType: FuelType,

open var owner: Owner

) : Trackable, Serviceable {

protected var odometer: Int = 0

private val _serviceHistory = mutableListOf<ServiceRecord>()

private var _currentLocation = "Unknown"

protected var status: VehicleStatus = VehicleStatus.Parked

// Trackable implementation

override val vehicleId: String = "${brand}_${model}_${year}_${System.currentTimeMillis()}"

override fun getCurrentLocation() = _currentLocation

override fun updateLocation(location: String) {

_currentLocation = location

println("📍 ${displayName()} location: $location")

}

// Serviceable implementation

override fun getServiceHistory(): List<ServiceRecord> = _serviceHistory.toList()

override fun addServiceRecord(record: ServiceRecord) {

_serviceHistory.add(record)

odometer = maxOf(odometer, record.odometer)

println("🔧 Service record added for ${displayName()}: ${record.type}")

}

override fun isDueForService(): Boolean {

val lastService = _serviceHistory.maxByOrNull { it.odometer }

return lastService == null || (odometer - lastService.odometer) >= 5000

}

// Abstract methods — each vehicle type implements differently

abstract fun displayName(): String

abstract fun getMaxSpeed(): Int

abstract fun calculateTax(): Double

abstract fun fuelEfficiency(): Double // km per liter or per kWh

// Template method — fixed algorithm, steps can be overridden

fun startJourney(destination: String): Boolean {

println("\n🚀 Starting journey to $destination...")

if (!preStartCheck()) {

println("❌ Pre-start check failed!")

return false

}

status = VehicleStatus.Moving(0, destination)

updateLocation("En route to $destination")

println("✅ ${displayName()} is now heading to $destination")

return true

}

protected open fun preStartCheck(): Boolean {

println(" Checking status...")

return status is VehicleStatus.Parked

}

fun getStatus(): String = when (val s = status) {

is VehicleStatus.Parked -> "🅿️ Parked at $_currentLocation"

is VehicleStatus.Moving -> "🚗 Moving at ${s.speed} km/h towards ${s.direction}"

is VehicleStatus.InService -> "🔧 In service: ${s.serviceType} (${s.estimatedHours}h)"

is VehicleStatus.Broken -> "🚨 Broken: ${s.issue} [${s.severity}]"

}

fun printSummary() {

println("""

            ╔══════════════════════════════════════╗
            ║         VEHICLE SUMMARY              ║
            ╠══════════════════════════════════════╣
            ║ Name: ${displayName().padEnd(32)}║
            ║ Owner: ${owner.name.padEnd(31)}║
            ║ Fuel: ${fuelType.label.padEnd(32)}║
            ║ Odometer: ${odometer.toString().padEnd(28)}║
            ║ Max Speed: ${getMaxSpeed().toString().padEnd(27)}║
            ║ Tax: ₹${calculateTax().toString().padEnd(31)}║
            ║ Service Due: ${isDueForService().toString().padEnd(25)}║
            ║ Status: ${getStatus().take(28).padEnd(29)}║
            ╚══════════════════════════════════════╝

""".trimIndent())

}

// Companion Object — class-level utilities

companion object {

private var totalVehicles = 0

fun getTotalCount() = totalVehicles

fun register() {

totalVehicles++

println("Vehicle #$totalVehicles registered")

}

}

init {

Vehicle.register()

}

}

// --- CONCRETE CLASSES ---

class Car(

brand: String,

model: String,

year: Int,

fuelType: FuelType,

owner: Owner,

private val numberOfDoors: Int = 4,

private val transmission: String = "Manual"

) : Vehicle(brand, model, year, fuelType, owner), Insurable {

private var _insuranceNumber = ""

private var _expiryDate = ""

private val fuelRecords = mutableListOf<FuelRecord>()

// Insurable implementation

override val insuranceNumber: String get() = _insuranceNumber

override val expiryDate: String get() = _expiryDate

override fun isInsuranceValid() = _expiryDate.isNotBlank()

override fun renewInsurance(newExpiry: String) {

_expiryDate = newExpiry

println("🛡️ Insurance renewed until $newExpiry")

}

override fun displayName() = "$year $brand $model ($numberOfDoors-door, $transmission)"

override fun getMaxSpeed() = if (fuelType == FuelType.ELECTRIC) 200 else 180

override fun calculateTax() = when (fuelType) {

FuelType.ELECTRIC -> 0.0

FuelType.HYBRID -> 5000.0

FuelType.CNG -> 8000.0

else -> 15000.0

}

override fun fuelEfficiency() = when (fuelType) {

FuelType.PETROL -> 15.0

FuelType.DIESEL -> 18.0

FuelType.ELECTRIC -> 6.0 // km per kWh

else -> 20.0

}

fun addFuelRecord(record: FuelRecord) {

fuelRecords.add(record)

println("⛽ Fuel record added: ${record.liters}L for ₹${record.totalCost}")

}

fun getTotalFuelCost() = fuelRecords.sumOf { it.totalCost }

fun setInsurance(number: String, expiry: String) {

_insuranceNumber = number

_expiryDate = expiry

println("🛡️ Insurance set: $number, valid till $expiry")

}

}

class Truck(

brand: String,

model: String,

year: Int,

owner: Owner,

private val cargoCapacityTons: Double

) : Vehicle(brand, model, year, FuelType.DIESEL, owner) {

private var currentCargoTons: Double = 0.0

override fun displayName() = "$year $brand $model (${cargoCapacityTons}T Truck)"

override fun getMaxSpeed() = if (currentCargoTons > cargoCapacityTons * 0.8) 60 else 80

override fun calculateTax() = cargoCapacityTons * 5000

override fun fuelEfficiency() = 8.0 // trucks are less efficient

override fun preStartCheck(): Boolean {

val baseCheck = super.preStartCheck()

val cargoCheck = currentCargoTons <= cargoCapacityTons

if (!cargoCheck) println(" ⚠️ Overloaded! ${currentCargoTons}T > ${cargoCapacityTons}T capacity")

return baseCheck && cargoCheck

}

fun loadCargo(tons: Double): Boolean {

return if (currentCargoTons + tons <= cargoCapacityTons) {

currentCargoTons += tons

println("📦 Loaded ${tons}T | Total: ${currentCargoTons}/${cargoCapacityTons}T")

true

} else {

println("❌ Cannot load! Capacity: ${cargoCapacityTons}T, Current: ${currentCargoTons}T")

false

}

}

fun unloadCargo() {

println("📦 Unloaded ${currentCargoTons}T")

currentCargoTons = 0.0

}

}

class ElectricScooter(

brand: String,

model: String,

year: Int,

owner: Owner,

private val batteryCapacityKwh: Double

) : Vehicle(brand, model, year, FuelType.ELECTRIC, owner) {

private var batteryLevel: Int = 100

override fun displayName() = "$year $brand $model (${batteryCapacityKwh}kWh Scooter)"

override fun getMaxSpeed() = 80

override fun calculateTax() = 0.0 // EV tax exempt

override fun fuelEfficiency() = 70.0 // 70 km per charge

override fun preStartCheck(): Boolean {

val baseCheck = super.preStartCheck()

if (batteryLevel < 10) println(" 🔋 Low battery! ${batteryLevel}%")

return baseCheck && batteryLevel > 5

}

fun charge(percentage: Int = 100) {

batteryLevel = minOf(100, batteryLevel + percentage)

println("🔋 Battery charged to $batteryLevel%")

}

fun getRangeEstimate() = (batteryLevel / 100.0 * fuelEfficiency()).toInt()

}

// --- EXTENSION FUNCTIONS ---

fun Vehicle.generateReport(): String {

return buildString {

appendLine("=== Vehicle Report ===")

appendLine("Vehicle: ${displayName()}")

appendLine("Owner: ${owner.name} (${owner.phone})")

appendLine("Location: ${getCurrentLocation()}")

appendLine("Status: ${getStatus()}")

appendLine("Service Due: ${if (isDueForService()) "⚠️ YES" else "✅ No"}")

appendLine("Eco Friendly: ${if (fuelType.isEcoFriendly) "🌱 Yes" else "🏭 No"}")

val history = getServiceHistory()

appendLine("Service Records: ${history.size}")

if (history.isNotEmpty()) {

appendLine("Last Service: ${history.last().date} — ${history.last().type}")

}

}

}

fun List<Vehicle>.totalTax() = sumOf { it.calculateTax() }

fun List<Vehicle>.byFuelType(type: FuelType) = filter { it.fuelType == type }

fun List<Vehicle>.dueForService() = filter { it.isDueForService() }

// --- VEHICLE MANAGEMENT SYSTEM ---

class VehicleManagementSystem {

private val vehicles = mutableListOf<Vehicle>()

fun addVehicle(vehicle: Vehicle) {

vehicles.add(vehicle)

println("✅ Added: ${vehicle.displayName()}")

}

fun removeVehicle(vehicleId: String): Boolean {

return vehicles.removeAll { it.vehicleId == vehicleId }.also {

if (it) println("✅ Vehicle removed") else println("❌ Vehicle not found")

}

}

fun findByOwner(ownerName: String): List<Vehicle> {

return vehicles.filter { it.owner.name.contains(ownerName, ignoreCase = true) }

}

fun printFleetReport() {

        println("\n" + "═".repeat(50))

println(" FLEET MANAGEMENT REPORT")

        println("═".repeat(50))

println("Total Vehicles: ${vehicles.size}")

println("Total Tax Liability: ₹${vehicles.totalTax()}")

println("Vehicles Due for Service: ${vehicles.dueForService().size}")

println("\nBy Fuel Type:")

FuelType.values().forEach { fuelType ->

val count = vehicles.byFuelType(fuelType).size

if (count > 0) println(" ${fuelType.label}: $count vehicles")

}

        println("═".repeat(50))

}

fun serviceReminder() {

val due = vehicles.dueForService()

if (due.isEmpty()) {

println("✅ No vehicles due for service!")

} else {

println("\n⚠️ Vehicles due for service:")

due.forEach { println(" - ${it.displayName()} (Owner: ${it.owner.name})") }

}

}

}

// --- MAIN FUNCTION ---

fun main() {

println("🏢 Initializing Vehicle Management System...")

val vms = VehicleManagementSystem()

// Create owners

val rahul = Owner("O001", "Rahul Sharma", "9876543210", "rahul@email.com")

val priya = Owner("O002", "Priya Singh", "9123456789", "priya@email.com")

val logistics = Owner("O003", "Sharma Logistics", "9999888877", "logistics@email.com")

// Create vehicles

val car1 = Car("Toyota", "Camry", 2022, FuelType.PETROL, rahul, 4, "Automatic").also {

it.setInsurance("INS001", "2025-12-31")

it.addServiceRecord(ServiceRecord("2023-01-15", "Oil Change", 2500.0, "Regular service", 15000))

}

val car2 = Car("Tata", "Nexon EV", 2023, FuelType.ELECTRIC, priya).also {

it.addFuelRecord(FuelRecord("2023-06-01", 25.0, 8.0, 5000))

}

val truck = Truck("Ashok Leyland", "Boss", 2020, logistics, 12.5).also {

it.loadCargo(8.0)

}

val scooter = ElectricScooter("Ola", "S1 Pro", 2023, rahul, 3.97)

// Add to system

vms.addVehicle(car1)

vms.addVehicle(car2)

vms.addVehicle(truck)

vms.addVehicle(scooter)

// Operations

car1.updateLocation("Mumbai, MH")

car1.startJourney("Pune")

truck.startJourney("Delhi")

scooter.charge(80)

println("Scooter range: ${scooter.getRangeEstimate()} km")

// Reports

vms.printFleetReport()

vms.serviceReminder()

// Extension function usage

println("\n" + car1.generateReport())

// Polymorphism — treat all as Vehicle

val allVehicles: List<Vehicle> = listOf(car1, car2, truck, scooter)

println("\nAll vehicle names:")

allVehicles.forEach { println(" ${it.displayName()} — Tax: ₹${it.calculateTax()}") }

// Statistics

println("\nTotal registered vehicles: ${Vehicle.getTotalCount()}")

}

SECTION 6
INTERVIEW MASTERY

Top 50 OOP Interview Questions with Answers

BASIC (1-15)

Q1. OOP ke 4 pillars kya hain? A: Encapsulation, Inheritance, Polymorphism, Abstraction.
Q2. Kotlin mein class by default final kyun hoti hai? A: "Design for inheritance or prohibit it" (Effective Java). Unexpected inheritance bugs prevent karta hai. open keyword explicitly inheritance allow karta hai — conscious decision.
Q3. data class mein equals() kaise kaam karta hai? A: Structural equality — primary constructor mein defined sab properties compare karta hai. == use karo structural ke liye, === reference equality ke liye.
Q4. Interface aur Abstract class mein main difference? A: Abstract class = single inheritance, constructor possible, state (stored properties) possible. Interface = multiple implementation, no constructor, no stored state (Kotlin mein). Abstract class "IS-A", Interface "CAN-DO".
Q5. lateinit aur lazy mein kya difference hai? A: lateinit = var, non-null, manually initialize later. lazy = val, initialized on first access, thread-safe by default. Use lateinit for DI/Android contexts, lazy for expensive computations.
Q6. open keyword kab use karte hain? A: Class ko inheritable banane ke liye, ya method/property ko overridable banane ke liye. Default mein sab final hote hain Kotlin mein.
Q7. override keyword mandatory kyun hai Kotlin mein? A: Explicitly show karta hai developer ka intent — "Yeh method intentionally parent ko override kar raha hai." Accidental override prevent karta hai (Java mein @Override optional tha).
Q8. Kotlin mein multiple inheritance kaise achieve karte hain? A: Multiple interfaces implement karke. Single class inheritance allowed hai, lekin multiple interfaces allowed hain.
Q9. Companion object kya hai? A: Class ke saath associated singleton object. Java's static members ki tarah. ClassName.method() se access. Factory methods, constants, utility functions ke liye.
Q10. object keyword ke kya uses hain? A:

Singleton: object MySingleton { }

Companion: companion object { }

Anonymous object: object : Interface { }

Q11. Sealed class kab use karte hain? A: Jab possible subtypes limited aur known hon. when expression ke saath perfect — else branch ki zarurat nahi. State machines, UI states, API results ke liye best.
Q12. Extension function actual class modify karta hai? A: Nahi! Compile time pe static function mein translate hoti hai. Class bytecode mein koi change nahi. Caller object this ke roop mein pass hota hai.
Q13. inner aur nested class mein kya difference? A: inner class outer class ke members access kar sakti hai (holds reference). nested class independent hai, outer members access nahi kar sakti (static-like). Memory: inner outer ka reference rakhti hai (memory leak risk), nested nahi.
Q14. super keyword ke uses? A:

Parent constructor call: super(params)

Parent method call: super.method()

Ambiguous interface methods: super<InterfaceName>.method()

Q15. Data class mein copy() kyun useful hai? A: Immutability maintain karte hue modification karna. Original object change nahi hota — new object milta hai. Functional programming pattern. val updated = user.copy(email = "new@email.com")

INTERMEDIATE (16-30)

Q16. Kotlin mein is aur as operators kya hain?
Kotlin
// 'is' — type check (smart cast!)
if (animal is Dog) {
    animal.bark()  // Auto cast! No explicit cast needed
}

// 'as' — explicit cast (throws exception if fails)
val dog = animal as Dog

// 'as?' — safe cast (returns null if fails)

val dog = animal as? Dog // dog: Dog? — null if not a Dog

Q17. Method overloading vs overriding difference? A:

Overloading (Compile-time): Same class, same name, different parameters. Which method = decided at compile time.

Overriding (Runtime): Parent-child, same signature. Which implementation = decided at runtime via vtable.

Q18. Any class kya hai Kotlin mein? A: Kotlin ka root class — har class implicitly Any se inherit karti hai (Java ke Object jaisi). equals(), hashCode(), toString() provide karta hai.
Q19. Property delegation kaise kaam karta hai? A: by keyword se property access (get/set) delegate class ko forward hoti hai. Delegate class mein getValue() aur setValue() operator functions hote hain. Built-in delegates: lazy, observable, vetoable, map.
Q20. Abstract class mein constructor kyun allowed hai? A: Child classes initialization ke liye. Jab child ka constructor call hota hai, super() through parent constructor run hota hai. Common initialization logic yahan rakh sakte hain.
Q21. Enum class mein kya kya ho sakta hai? A: Properties, methods, abstract methods, companion objects, interfaces implement karna — sab! Har enum constant ek object hai us enum class ka.
Q22. Nothing type kya hai Kotlin mein? A: Ek type jo kabhi value produce nahi karta. throw expression ka type Nothing hai. Functions jo always throw ya infinite loop mein hain unka return type Nothing hota hai. Nothing is a subtype of every type.
Kotlin
fun fail(msg: String): Nothing = throw IllegalStateException(msg)
fun infiniteLoop(): Nothing = while (true) { }
Q23. Sealed class vs enum mein key difference? A: Enum = each constant same structure. Sealed = each subclass different structure, different data. Sealed subclasses multiple instances ban sakti hain (data classes), enums sirf defined constants.

Q24. init blocks ka order kya hota hai? A: Top to bottom, property initializations ke saath interleaved. Primary constructor → Properties + init blocks (in order) → Secondary constructor body (last).

Q25. Visibility modifier internal Java mein kya hai? A: Java mein internal nahi hai (Java has package-private without modifier). Kotlin ka internal module-level — module ke bahar accessible nahi. Library/SDK development mein useful.
Q26. Extension function vs member function — kya prefer karein? A:

Member function: Core behavior, state access chahiye, override karna ho

Extension function: Utility, third-party classes extend karna, keep class lean

// Good extension — utility, doesn't need internal state

fun String.isPalindrome() = this == this.reversed()

// Bad extension — should be member (needs internal state)

// fun User.getInternalData() = _privateData // Can't access private!

Q27. Abstract method aur interface method mein difference? A: Abstract method abstract class mein hota hai, interface method interface mein. Kotlin mein dono default implementation rakh sakte hain. Abstract class mein access modifiers laga sakte ho, interface mein default public.
Q28. Smart cast kab kaam karta hai? A: Jab compiler guarantee kar sake ki type nahi badlegi — local variables ke saath (immutable ya checked). var properties ke saath nahi (doosri thread change kar sakti hai).
Kotlin
fun smartCast(obj: Any) {
    if (obj is String) {
        println(obj.length)  // Smart cast! obj treated as String here
    }
    
    val str: Any = "Hello"
    if (str is String) {
        println(str.uppercase())  // Smart cast works!
    }
}
Q29. reified type parameter kya hai? A: Generic functions mein type information runtime pe available karna. Normally generics type-erased hote hain JVM pe. reified + inline se type retain hota hai.
Kotlin
inline fun <reified T> isInstance(value: Any) = value is T
println(isInstance<String>("Hello"))  // true
println(isInstance<Int>("Hello"))     // false
Q30. Liskov Substitution Principle (LSP) kya hai? A: "Child class parent class ki jagah use ho sake bina program break kiye." Matlab child class parent ke CONTRACT ko follow kare — weak preconditions, strong postconditions.
Kotlin
// ✅ LSP satisfied
open class Bird { open fun fly() = println("Flying") }
class Eagle : Bird() { override fun fly() = println("Eagle soaring!") }
// Eagle CAN fly — substitutable!

// ❌ LSP violated
open class Rectangle(open var width: Double, open var height: Double) {
    open fun area() = width * height
}
class Square(side: Double) : Rectangle(side, side) {
    override var width: Double
        get() = super.width
        set(value) { super.width = value; super.height = value }  // Breaks Rectangle contract!
}
ADVANCED (31-50)
Q31. Diamond problem Kotlin mein kaise solve hota hai?
Kotlin
interface A { fun hello() = println("A") }
interface B { fun hello() = println("B") }

class C : A, B {
    override fun hello() {
        super<A>.hello()  // Must specify which parent
        super<B>.hello()
        println("C's hello")
    }
}
Q32. Delegation pattern kab prefer karo inheritance ke upar? A: "Favor composition over inheritance" — jab "IS-A" relationship nahi, sirf functionality chahiye. Runtime mein delegate change kar sako (strategy pattern). Inheritance changes = breaking changes. Delegation mein sirf delegate change karo.

Q33. Object expressions (anonymous objects) ka use?
Kotlin
// One-time interface implementation
val listener = object : View.OnClickListener {
    override fun onClick(view: View) {
        println("Clicked!")
    }
}

// Can implement multiple interfaces
val combined = object : Runnable, Closeable {
    override fun run() { println("Running") }
    override fun close() { println("Closing") }
}
Q34. Generic class with variance (in, out) kya hai?
Kotlin
// out = covariant (Producer) — T can be returned, not accepted
class Producer<out T>(private val value: T) {
    fun produce(): T = value
}

// in = contravariant (Consumer) — T can be accepted, not returned
class Consumer<in T> {
    fun consume(value: T) { println(value) }
}

val producer: Producer<Number> = Producer<Int>(42)  // Int is-a Number ✅
Q35. Sealed class mein subtypes same file mein kyun honi chahiye? A: Kotlin 1.5 tak same file/package required tha (strict). Ab same package ya module mein ho sakti hain. Main reason: compiler exhaustive when guarantee karne ke liye sabhi subtypes ko compile time pe jaanna chahiye.
Q36. operator fun kya hai? OOP se kaise related hai?
Kotlin
data class Vector(val x: Double, val y: Double) {
    operator fun plus(other: Vector) = Vector(x + other.x, y + other.y)
    operator fun times(scalar: Double) = Vector(x * scalar, y * scalar)
    operator fun unaryMinus() = Vector(-x, -y)
    
    override fun toString() = "Vector($x, $y)"
}

fun main() {
    val v1 = Vector(1.0, 2.0)
    val v2 = Vector(3.0, 4.0)
    println(v1 + v2)    // Vector(4.0, 6.0)
    println(v1 * 3.0)   // Vector(3.0, 6.0)
    println(-v1)        // Vector(-1.0, -2.0)
}
Q37. Coroutines ke saath OOP — ViewModel pattern?
Kotlin
// Simplified ViewModel with sealed state
class UserViewModel {
    sealed class State {
        object Loading : State()
        data class Success(val users: List<String>) : State()
        data class Error(val message: String) : State()
    }
    
    private var state: State = State.Loading
    
    fun loadUsers() {
        state = State.Loading
        // Simulate: state = State.Success(listOf("Rahul", "Priya"))
        state = State.Success(listOf("Rahul", "Priya", "Arjun"))
        renderState()
    }
    
    private fun renderState() = when (val s = state) {
        is State.Loading -> println("⏳ Loading...")
        is State.Success -> println("✅ ${s.users.size} users: ${s.users}")
        is State.Error -> println("❌ ${s.message}")
    }
}
Q38. Encapsulation violate hone ke examples?
Kotlin
// ❌ Public mutable collections
class Bad {
    val items = mutableListOf<String>()  // Anyone can add/remove!
}

// ✅ Controlled access
class Good {
    private val _items = mutableListOf<String>()
    val items: List<String> get() = _items.toList()  // Read-only view
    
    fun addItem(item: String) {
        if (item.isNotBlank()) _items.add(item)
    }
}
Q39. typealias OOP mein kaise help karta hai?
Kotlin
typealias UserId = Int
typealias ProductList = List<Product>
typealias OnSuccess<T> = (T) -> Unit
typealias OnError = (Exception) -> Unit

// Readable function signatures!
fun fetchUser(id: UserId, onSuccess: OnSuccess<User>, onError: OnError) { }
Q40. SOLID principles Kotlin mein?

S - Single Responsibility:
Kotlin
// ❌ UserManager does too much
class UserManager { fun createUser() {} fun sendEmail() {} fun saveToDb() {} }

// ✅ Each class has one job
class UserService { fun createUser() {} }
class EmailService { fun sendWelcomeEmail(user: User) {} }
class UserRepository { fun save(user: User) {} }
O - Open/Closed:
Kotlin
// Open for extension, closed for modification
interface Discount { fun apply(price: Double): Double }
class SeasonalDiscount : Discount { override fun apply(price: Double) = price * 0.8 }
class LoyaltyDiscount : Discount { override fun apply(price: Double) = price * 0.9 }

// Add new discount type without modifying existing code!

L - Liskov Substitution: (Covered in Q30)

I - Interface Segregation:

// ❌ Fat interface — forces implementing useless methods

interface Worker { fun work(); fun eat(); fun sleep() }

// ✅ Segregated

interface Workable { fun work() }

interface Eatable { fun eat() }

class Robot : Workable { override fun work() = println("Working") }

class Human : Workable, Eatable { override fun work() {} override fun eat() {} }

D - Dependency Inversion:

Kotlin
// Depend on abstraction, not concretions
interface MessageSender { fun send(msg: String) }
class EmailSender : MessageSender { override fun send(msg: String) = println("Email: $msg") }
class SmsSender : MessageSender { override fun send(msg: String) = println("SMS: $msg") }

class NotificationService(private val sender: MessageSender) {  // Abstraction!
    fun notify(msg: String) = sender.send(msg)
}
// Inject any sender!
val service = NotificationService(EmailSender())
Q41-50: Tricky Scenarios

Q41: Output kya hoga?
Kotlin
open class Base {
    open val name: String = "Base"
    init { println("Hello from ${name}") }  // ← Tricky!
}

class Derived : Base() {
    override val name: String = "Derived"
}

fun main() {
    val d = Derived()
    // Output: "Hello from null" ← NOT "Derived"!
}

// Reason: init runs before Derived's name is initialized!

// name override Derived mein null/default hai jab Base's init runs

Q42: Can you override val with var?
Kotlin
open class Parent { open val x: Int = 10 }
class Child : Parent() { override var x: Int = 20 }  // ✅ val → var allowed!
// But var → val NOT allowed (narrowing visibility)
Q43: Companion object inheritance?
Kotlin
open class Animal {
    companion object {
        fun create() = println("Animal created")
    }
}
class Dog : Animal()
// Dog.create()  // ❌ NOT accessible! Companion not inherited
Animal.create()  // ✅ Only through Animal
Q44: Interface default method + class member — which wins?
Kotlin
interface Greeter { fun greet() = println("Interface greet") }
open class Person { fun greet() = println("Class greet") }
class User : Person(), Greeter
// val u = User()
// u.greet()  // Output: "Class greet" — class member wins over interface default!
Q45: Data class inheritance?
Kotlin
// data class cannot be open (since Kotlin 1.1+)
// data class Parent(val x: Int) — cannot be subclassed by another data class

// But data class can extend non-data open class or implement interfaces!

open class Base(val id: Int)

data class Child(val name: String, val baseId: Int) : Base(baseId)

SECTION 7
PRACTICE & ASSIGNMENTS

Beginner Problems

B1: Library Book System

Kotlin
data class Book(val isbn: String, val title: String, val author: String, val year: Int)

class Library {
    private val _books = mutableListOf<Book>()
    private val borrowedBooks = mutableMapOf<String, String>() // isbn → borrower name
    
    val availableBooks: List<Book>
        get() = _books.filter { it.isbn !in borrowedBooks }
    
    fun addBook(book: Book) {
        _books.add(book)
        println("📚 Added: '${book.title}' by ${book.author}")
    }
    
    fun borrowBook(isbn: String, borrowerName: String): Boolean {
        val book = _books.find { it.isbn == isbn }
        return when {
            book == null -> { println("❌ Book not found!"); false }
            isbn in borrowedBooks -> { println("❌ Already borrowed by ${borrowedBooks[isbn]}!"); false }
            else -> {
                borrowedBooks[isbn] = borrowerName
                println("✅ '${book.title}' borrowed by $borrowerName")
                true
            }
        }
    }
    
    fun returnBook(isbn: String): Boolean {
        val borrower = borrowedBooks.remove(isbn) ?: return false.also { println("❌ Not borrowed!") }
        val book = _books.find { it.isbn == isbn }
        println("✅ '${book?.title}' returned by $borrower")
        return true
    }
    
    fun searchByAuthor(author: String) = _books.filter { it.author.contains(author, true) }
    
    fun printStatus() {
        println("=== Library Status ===")
        println("Total: ${_books.size} | Available: ${availableBooks.size} | Borrowed: ${borrowedBooks.size}")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val lib = Library()
    lib.addBook(Book("978001", "Clean Code", "Robert Martin", 2008))
    lib.addBook(Book("978002", "Kotlin in Action", "Dmitry Jemerov", 2017))
    lib.addBook(Book("978003", "Effective Java", "Joshua Bloch", 2018))
    
    lib.borrowBook("978001", "Rahul")
    lib.borrowBook("978001", "Priya")  // Already borrowed
    lib.returnBook("978001")
    lib.borrowBook("978001", "Priya")  // Now available
    
    lib.printStatus()
}

Intermediate Problems

I1: E-commerce Order System

Kotlin
enum class OrderStatus { PENDING, CONFIRMED, SHIPPED, DELIVERED, CANCELLED }

data class Product(val id: Int, val name: String, val price: Double, var stock: Int)

data class OrderItem(val product: Product, val quantity: Int) {
    val subtotal: Double get() = product.price * quantity
}

sealed class PaymentResult {
    data class Success(val transactionId: String) : PaymentResult()
    data class Failure(val reason: String) : PaymentResult()
}

class Order(val orderId: String, val customerId: String) {
    private val _items = mutableListOf<OrderItem>()
    var status: OrderStatus = OrderStatus.PENDING
        private set
    
    val items: List<OrderItem> get() = _items.toList()
    val subtotal: Double get() = _items.sumOf { it.subtotal }
    val tax: Double get() = subtotal * 0.18
    val total: Double get() = subtotal + tax
    
    fun addItem(product: Product, quantity: Int): Boolean {
        if (status != OrderStatus.PENDING) {
            println("❌ Cannot modify confirmed order!")
            return false
        }
        if (product.stock < quantity) {
            println("❌ Insufficient stock for ${product.name}!")
            return false
        }
        _items.add(OrderItem(product, quantity))
        product.stock -= quantity
        println("✅ Added: ${product.name} x$quantity = ₹${product.price * quantity}")
        return true
    }
    
    fun processPayment(): PaymentResult {
        if (_items.isEmpty()) return PaymentResult.Failure("Cart is empty!")
        
        // Simulate payment processing
        val success = (1..10).random() > 2  // 80% success rate
        return if (success) {
            status = OrderStatus.CONFIRMED
            val txnId = "TXN${System.currentTimeMillis()}"
            println("✅ Payment successful! TXN: $txnId | Total: ₹$total")
            PaymentResult.Success(txnId)
        } else {
            PaymentResult.Failure("Payment gateway error")
        }
    }
    
    fun ship() {
        if (status == OrderStatus.CONFIRMED) {
            status = OrderStatus.SHIPPED
            println("🚚 Order $orderId shipped!")
        }
    }
    
    fun printInvoice() {
        println("\n=== Invoice: $orderId ===")
        _items.forEach { println("  ${it.product.name} x${it.quantity} = ₹${it.subtotal}") }
        println("Subtotal: ₹$subtotal | Tax: ₹$tax | Total: ₹$total")
        println("Status: $status")
    }
}

fun main() {
    val products = listOf(
        Product(1, "Phone", 15000.0, 10),
        Product(2, "Case", 500.0, 50),
        Product(3, "Charger", 800.0, 0)  // Out of stock!
    )
    
    val order = Order("ORD001", "CUST001")
    order.addItem(products[0], 1)
    order.addItem(products[1], 2)
    order.addItem(products[2], 1)  // Should fail — out of stock
    
    val result = order.processPayment()
    when (result) {
        is PaymentResult.Success -> { order.ship(); order.printInvoice() }
        is PaymentResult.Failure -> println("❌ Payment failed: ${result.reason}")
    }
}

Advanced Problems

A1: Design Pattern — Observer Pattern
Kotlin
interface Observer<T> {
    fun onUpdate(event: String, data: T)
}

abstract class Observable<T> {
    private val observers = mutableListOf<Observer<T>>()
    
    fun subscribe(observer: Observer<T>) {
        observers.add(observer)
        println("👀 Observer subscribed: ${observer::class.simpleName}")
    }
    
    fun unsubscribe(observer: Observer<T>) = observers.remove(observer)
    
    protected fun notify(event: String, data: T) {
        observers.forEach { it.onUpdate(event, data) }
    }
}

data class StockPrice(val symbol: String, val price: Double, val change: Double)

class StockMarket : Observable<StockPrice>() {
    private val prices = mutableMapOf<String, Double>()
    
    fun updatePrice(symbol: String, newPrice: Double) {
        val oldPrice = prices.getOrDefault(symbol, newPrice)
        val change = ((newPrice - oldPrice) / oldPrice) * 100
        prices[symbol] = newPrice
        
        val stockPrice = StockPrice(symbol, newPrice, change)
        println("\n📈 ${symbol}: ₹${newPrice} (${if (change >= 0) "+" else ""}${"%.2f".format(change)}%)")
        notify("PRICE_UPDATE", stockPrice)
    }
}

class Portfolio(private val name: String) : Observer<StockPrice> {
    private val holdings = mutableMapOf<String, Pair<Int, Double>>() // symbol → (qty, avgPrice)
    
    fun addHolding(symbol: String, quantity: Int, avgPrice: Double) {
        holdings[symbol] = Pair(quantity, avgPrice)
    }
    
    override fun onUpdate(event: String, data: StockPrice) {
        val holding = holdings[data.symbol] ?: return
        val (qty, avgPrice) = holding
        val currentValue = qty * data.price
        val invested = qty * avgPrice
        val pnl = currentValue - invested
        println("  💼 $name | ${data.symbol}: ${qty} shares | P&L: ₹${"%.0f".format(pnl)} (${if (pnl >= 0) "+" else ""}${"%.1f".format((pnl/invested)*100)}%)")
    }
}

class PriceAlert(private val symbol: String, private val targetPrice: Double) : Observer<StockPrice> {
    override fun onUpdate(event: String, data: StockPrice) {
        if (data.symbol == symbol && data.price >= targetPrice) {
            println("  🔔 ALERT! $symbol reached target ₹$targetPrice! Current: ₹${data.price}")
        }
    }
}

fun main() {
    val market = StockMarket()
    
    val portfolio1 = Portfolio("Rahul's Portfolio").apply {
        addHolding("TATA", 100, 800.0)
        addHolding("INFY", 50, 1200.0)
    }
    
    val portfolio2 = Portfolio("Priya's Portfolio").apply {
        addHolding("TATA", 200, 750.0)
    }
    
    val alert = PriceAlert("TATA", 900.0)
    
    market.subscribe(portfolio1)
    market.subscribe(portfolio2)
    market.subscribe(alert)
    
    market.updatePrice("TATA", 850.0)
    market.updatePrice("INFY", 1350.0)
    market.updatePrice("TATA", 910.0)  // Should trigger alert!
    
    market.unsubscribe(portfolio2)
    market.updatePrice("TATA", 920.0)  // portfolio2 won't get this
}

SECTION 8
SUMMARY + REVISION NOTES

Quick Revision Cheat Sheet

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                   OOP IN KOTLIN — CHEAT SHEET               ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ CORE CONCEPTS                                                ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ Class          → Blueprint: class Name(props) { methods }   ║
║ Object         → Instance: val obj = ClassName()            ║
║ Constructor    → Primary (header) | Secondary (body)        ║
║ Properties     → val (immutable) | var (mutable)            ║
║ Methods        → fun methodName(): ReturnType { }           ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ VISIBILITY                                                   ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ public  → Everywhere (default)                              ║
║ private → Same class only                                   ║
║ protected → Same class + subclasses                         ║
║ internal → Same module                                      ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ 4 PILLARS                                                    ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ Encapsulation  → private data + public methods              ║
║ Inheritance    → open class Parent | class Child : Parent() ║
║ Polymorphism   → override fun method() + parent reference   ║
║ Abstraction    → abstract class | interface                 ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ ADVANCED                                                     ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ data class    → Auto equals/hashCode/toString/copy          ║
║ sealed class  → Limited subtypes, exhaustive when           ║
║ enum class    → Fixed constants with properties/methods     ║
║ companion     → Class-level "static" members                ║
║ object        → Singleton (no new needed)                   ║
║ by            → Delegation keyword                          ║
║ lateinit      → var, initialize later                       ║
║ lazy          → val, initialize on first access             ║
║ extension fun → Add methods without subclassing             ║
║ inner class   → Access outer class members                  ║
║ nested class  → Independent of outer class                  ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ KEY COMPARISONS                                              ║
╠══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ Abstract vs Interface:                                       ║
║   Abstract = IS-A + state + single inherit                  ║
║   Interface = CAN-DO + multiple implement                   ║
║                                                              ║
║ Sealed vs Enum:                                              ║
║   Sealed = Different data per subtype, multiple instances   ║
║   Enum = Same structure, one instance per constant          ║
║                                                              ║
║ lateinit vs lazy:                                            ║
║   lateinit = var, set manually, no primitives               ║
║   lazy = val, set on first access, thread-safe              ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

Key Points to Remember

🔑 The Golden Rules:

"Design for inheritance or prohibit it" — open explicitly, else final by default

"Program to interfaces, not implementations" — Dependency Inversion

"Favor composition over inheritance" — Use delegation when possible

"Encapsulate what varies" — Private by default, expose only what's necessary

"Each class should have one reason to change" — Single Responsibility

🧠 Memory Tricks:

A-PIE = OOP Pillars

A = Abstraction

P = Polymorphism

I = Inheritance

E = Encapsulation

SOLID = S.O.L.I.D.

S = Single Responsibility

O = Open/Closed

L = Liskov Substitution

I = Interface Segregation

D = Dependency Inversion

⚡ Quick Decision Guide:

Data store karna hai (DTO/Model)? → data class

Fixed set of constants? → enum class

Limited known subtypes with different data? → sealed class

Global single instance? → object

Class-level utils/factory? → companion object

Multiple capabilities? → interfaces

Shared state + partial implementation? → abstract class

Third-party class extend karna? → extension function

Initialize later manually? → lateinit

Initialize once on first use? → lazy

Delegate behavior to another class? → by delegation

Mistakes to Avoid

❌Classes by default inheritable samajhna → ✅ Kotlin classes are final!
❌Sab kuch public rakhna → ✅ Private by default, expose carefully
❌init mein heavy work → ✅ Lazy initialization use karo
❌lateinit ko primitives pe use karna → ✅ Only works on non-primitive objects
❌Super call bhoolna → ✅ super.method() especially in lifecycle methods
❌Data class open banana → ✅ Data classes cannot be open
❌Interface mein state store karna → ✅ Interfaces can't have backing fields
❌Deep inheritance (5+ levels) → ✅ Max 2-3 levels, prefer composition
❌Circular dependencies → ✅ Dependency Inversion use karo
❌God class banana (500+ lines) → ✅ Single Responsibility principle
❌lateinit check karna bhoolna → ✅ ::prop.isInitialized use karo
❌Mutable collections expose karna → ✅ Return List not MutableList
